Customs here takes a chip's origin to be its place of diffusion and collects the tax that follows from it (the decision does not name the duty): an electronics company in Xiamen declared integrated circuits under tariff line 854232 in several lots; four lots declared as of Thai origin were found to be of US origin by the place of diffusion, the company made up the unpaid tax, "当事人在海关发现违法行为后主动补缴漏缴税款" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext118], and was fined. Words: "集同关缉违字〔2026〕12号" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext118]; "当事人以一般贸易方式向海关申报进口税则号列854232项下集成电路等货物多批次" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext118]; "等4票货物申报原产地为泰国，申报总价共计人民币57.96万元，实际产地（扩散地）为美国" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext118] (declared origin Thailand, declared value 579,600 yuan in all, actual place of production, the place of diffusion, the United States); "漏缴税款人民币654933.86元" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext118]; "科处罚款人民币20.15万元" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext118]; three more lots declared as Philippine were found to be French by the same test, "未产生税率差" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext118] (no difference in the rate). The decision names no maker and no model; 854232 is the line that a trade column of April 2025 gives as the memory line, the one kind its account leaves under the duty: "只有存储作用 854232" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext12-wayback] (Tier 2, context). It stands beside the Ningbo and Liantang decisions in this section; like them it is about a general rule, here the declaration of origin, not about Nvidia or AI chips. Cuts: CONTEXT (limit 2: the published measure against American chips is a duty by origin, and customs applies it by the wafer's place of diffusion; a chip made at a foundry outside the United States is outside it).

### 2.5 Named officials

- Zhuang Rongwen, head of the cyberspace regulator, Aug 2024: "着力促进自主可控算力芯片、算法框架研发建设" (work hard to promote the research, development and building of independently controllable computing chips and algorithm frameworks) [cn-factions-zhuang-interview-20240812].
- Chen Yixin, Minister of State Security, signed article, Sept 2026: "确保关键核心技术自主可控,牢牢掌握技术主权" (ensure that key core technologies are independently controllable; hold technological sovereignty firmly in hand) [cn-factions-chen-ai-202609].
- Li Chao, of the state planner's policy research office, at its press conference of May 22, 2026 ("李超 2026/05/22 政研室" [cn-policy-ndrc-chip-adaptation-2026]; the page does not call him spokesperson: 发言人 0 places, against 7 on a foreign ministry page; Xinhua's account of the same press conference, on gov.cn, gives him as "国家发展改革委政策研究室副主任、新闻发言人李超" (deputy director of the planner's policy research office and its spokesman) [cn-le28-govcn-ndrc-briefing-20260522]): "指导国产大模型加大力度适配国产算力芯片" (guiding domestic large models to step up adaptation to domestic computing chips) [cn-policy-ndrc-chip-adaptation-2026].
- Zheng Shanjie, head of the NDRC, May 2026: "全链条推进关键核心技术攻关" (advance key core technology research along the whole chain) [cn-factions-zheng-ndrc-20260509].
- **Oct 31, 2025 · Li Chao, of the state planner's policy research office, at its press conference** (an official on the record). New large and very large computing facilities are to be sited preferably within the national hub clusters; the answer has no chip rule. Words: "新建大型和超大型算力设施优先布局在全国一体化算力网国家枢纽算力设施集群范围内" [cn-le14-ndrc-presser-20251031]; "强化“东数西算”规划布局刚性约束，合理规划、严格把关" [cn-le14-ndrc-presser-20251031]; "2025/10/31   政研室" [cn-le14-ndrc-presser-20251031]. This is the term the window-guidance paper of the same day uses (Tier 2). Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Dec 31, 2025 · Li Chao, at the state planner's press conference for December** (an official on the record; the planner's own transcript). Asked how good home-made computing is, he said home-made chips were being adapted fast in different uses with very good results, and that super-node cluster links gave home-made computing a good chance to catch up with the world's leaders; he named no foreign chip and no approval (英伟达 0, H200 0, 进口 0 on the transcript). Words: "目前，国产芯片产品在不同场景中加速适配，应用成效可以说非常好" [cn-ga15-ndrc-presser-20251231-json]; "定于12月31日（星期三）上午10:00，召开国家发展改革委12月份新闻发布会" [cn-ga15-ndrc-presser-20251231]. Cuts: FOR (the direction, in public); CONTEXT for motive (none stated).
- **Jun 18, 2026 · Li Chao, spokesman of the state planner, at its press conference, asked about the foreign report of a large computing-network investment** (an official on the record; the planner's own transcript). He described the existing layout and stock of capacity, gave no investment figure and no home-made share, and said market forces would play the decisive role. Words: "近期有外媒报道中国政府正在研究大规模投资建设算力网。请问能否介绍一下相关情况？" [cn-le27-ndrc-presser-20260618-json] (the question); "截至到今年3月底，我国已建成智能算力规模188.2万P" [cn-le27-ndrc-presser-20260618-json]; "在具体建设过程中，市场力量将起决定性作用；我们将会同有关部门，加强统筹协调和政策引导。" [cn-le27-ndrc-presser-20260618-json] (in actual construction, market forces will play the decisive role; we will strengthen coordination and policy guidance with the departments concerned). Neither confirms nor denies the reported draft of 80% (Tier 2). Cuts: CONTEXT, leaning AGAINST a state-mandated share.
- **Jul 31, 2026 · Jiang Yi, director of the state planner's policy research office, on the six networks and, in a separate answer, on AI** (an official on the record). Tens of thousands of home-made AI chips run at the Zhengzhou node; the first fully home-made 100,000-card cluster is in use; computing is built mainly by corporate investment. Cuts: FOR on direction; AGAINST a mandate.

China Telecom is building four fully home-made 10,000-card clusters. Words: "在京津冀、长三角、粤港澳等区域打造四个全国产化万卡智算集群" [cn-le17-cs-ct-domestic-clusters-20260913]. Cuts: FOR (the state operators' side of the split in section 3).
- **Sept 2026 · Chen Yixin, Party secretary and Minister of State Security, signed article in 《中国网信》 2026 issue 8, read whole** (a named official's published text; held on Nankai University's site, Sept 18, and in the People's Forum copy of Sept 14, which gives the magazine and issue; the record so far quotes one sentence of it). It has two sentences on chips: where it sums up Xi Jinping's statements on AI, a call to concentrate effort on core technologies such as high-end chips and basic software ("集中力量攻克高端芯片、基础软件等核心技术" [cn-factions-chen-ai-202609]); and, among its own measures, a call for research, set beside data pools and the computing network; among the risks of AI it lists other countries' entity lists and technology controls that strictly limit other countries in bringing in frontier equipment, and users who put sensitive information into foreign AI products; under international cooperation it opposes technology barriers. It names no foreign firm and gives buyers no instruction (英伟达 0, 国产 0 in the Nankai copy; control: 芯片 2). Words: "来源: 《中国网信》2026年第8期 作者: 陈一新 国家安全部党委书记、部长" [cn-ga31-mssposts-15]; "加大对人工智能领域基础原理和算力芯片、开发框架、应用软件等关键核心技术的科研攻关" [cn-factions-chen-ai-202609] (step up research to tackle the basic principles of AI and key core technologies such as computing chips, development frameworks and application software); "通过制定实体清单、实施技术管制、垄断行业标准、建立闭源生态等手段,严格限制相关国家引进前沿技术设备" [cn-factions-chen-ai-202609] (by means such as entity lists, technology controls, monopolised industry standards and closed-source ecosystems, strictly limit the countries concerned from bringing in frontier technology and equipment); "使用境外人工智能产品处理敏感信息和向境外输出数据" [cn-factions-chen-ai-202609]; "坚决抵制霸权主义、技术壁垒、封闭排他" [cn-factions-chen-ai-202609]. Cuts: FOR on direction (self-reliance in computing chips as a security aim), AGAINST a reading that the minister told companies what to buy; CONTEXT for limit 4 (he names foreign controls as what strictly limits the bringing in of frontier equipment).
- **Aug 14, 2025 to Oct 20, 2026 · Officials of the state planner, the industry ministry, the data administration and the state-assets regulator at their press conferences: home-made chips advanced, adapted and offered free; none speaks of Nvidia, of chip imports or of chip approvals** (named officials on the record, the bodies' own transcripts except where said; found through searches on Nov 25, 2026, not a census: the State Council Information Office's index did not open to the search tool). The state planner's Li Chao, Sept 29, 2025: "在“两重”建设中支持国产算力、模型、语料库发展" [cn-ga31-cndocs-45] (support home-made computing, models and corpora in the «two major» programmes); its Jiang Yi, Jul 31, 2026: "首个全国产10万卡人工智能超集群正式投用", and "深度求索、月之暗面等本土企业先后发布多个万亿级参数开源大模型，国产大模型全球总下载量突破100亿次，国产模型与国产算力芯片加速适配" [cn-ga31-sciondrc-2]. The industry ministry's vice minister Zhang Yunming, asked by CNBC on Jan 21, 2026 "如何平衡创新发展可能带来的失业或是外国限制？", answered with home results and named no foreign restriction: "国内企业发布多款人工智能芯片产品，智能算力规模达1590EFLOPS" [cn-ga31-sciondrc-1] (the ministry's page gives its source as "来源：中国网、工信微报" [cn-ga31-sciondrc-1]); its "工业和信息化部总工程师王卫明", Oct 20, 2026 (the transcript as an industry association reprints it): "国产算力与大模型适配能力不断增强" [cn-ga31-sciondrc-43]. The data administration's head Liu Liehong, Aug 14, 2025: "过去业界常说“缺芯少魂”，形象反映了我国在高端芯片、操作系统等方面自主可控不足的问题" [cn-ga31-sciondrc-32]. The state-assets regulator's Pang Xiaogang, Jan 28, 2026 (a provincial daily's transcript): central firms' computing platforms are "积极适配全品类国产芯片", and a community that, in Pang's words, «we» set up offers "免费提供国产智算芯片超过2000张" [cn-ga31-sciondrc-24]; Sept 28, 2026 (a city government's reprint of the transcript): "累计建成投产6个国产万卡智算集群" [cn-ga31-sciondrc-57]. The searches of these bodies' transcripts for 英伟达, H20, H200 and 进口 found no answer on Nvidia's chips (control: the commerce ministry's answer of Jul 16, 2026 is found by the same route). Cuts: FOR on direction (officials praise and fund home-made chips), CONTEXT (no official at these press conferences speaks of Nvidia, of import approvals or of a rule on chips).
- **Sept 9, 2025 to Nov 3, 2026 · Five more officials at press conferences, on home-made chips and on what government buys: research, free cards, promotion of home-made products; again nothing on Nvidia, chip imports or chip approvals** (named officials on the record, the bodies' own transcripts except where said; adds to the entry above from a second search of Nov 25, 2026, still not a census). The industry ministry's vice minister Zhang Yunming, Sept 9, 2025, asked by a foreign reporter "在工厂部署AI技术的需求与确保它不会最终摧毁蓝领制造业工作岗位之间，如何取得平衡？" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-60]: "加快高端算力芯片、工业多模态算法、软硬件适配等技术攻关" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-60] (speed up the tackling of technologies such as high-end computing chips, industrial multimodal algorithms and the fit of software to hardware). The science ministry's copy, credited to 国新网, of the transcript of the briefing of "2025年10月24日" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-20] on the Central Committee's recommendations (the saved copy labels the answer as the science minister Yin Hejun's: "请阴和俊同志回答" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-20]): "聚力开发新的模型算法、高端算力芯片" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-20]. The state-assets regulator's deputy head Li Zhen ("副主任李镇先生" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-48]), Sept 17, 2025 (the transcript as a central state firm's site carries it): its AI community, "面向广大青年创业者、科技爱好者、高校院所等，免费提供国产智算芯片超过2000张" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-48] (more than 2,000 home-made AI computing chips offered free to young founders, technology enthusiasts, universities, institutes and others), and "通信运营商加快向AI基础底座供应商转变，累计投资超百亿元，建成4个“万卡集群”" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-48]; he names no maker of the four clusters' cards. The state planner's Ren Zhongbao, Nov 3, 2026 ("发布时间：2026/11/03" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-71]), on the six new pillar industries, of which he names integrated circuits first: "我们将强化自主创新产品和技术推广应用，落实好首台（套）、首批次、首版次应用政策" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-71] (strengthen the promotion and use of independently innovated products and technologies, and carry out well the policies on the use of first units or sets, first batches and first versions). And the vice finance minister Liao Min, Jan 20, 2026, on two complaints from "European and American friends" that their products were kept out of government purchasing: "这两个相关产品，实际上均属于在国外生产的进口产品，而非在中国生产的本国产品" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-14], "因此，这个产品不在中国政府的采购范围里面，并不存在歧视性待遇" [cn-ga31-sciowhole-14] (the two products were imports made abroad, not domestic products made in China, and so outside the scope of government purchasing, and there is no discriminatory treatment; he names no product). Cuts: FOR (the declared line: research and public promotion of home-made chips), CONTEXT (limit 2: none is an instruction to companies, and none names Nvidia).

Asked what the city had achieved in artificial intelligence, Zhu named Nvidia's computing ecosystem as what Huawei's is benchmarked against and substitutes for, which he counted among the key progress of the five years to 2025. Words: "2026年2月10日上午，深圳市七届人大七次会议举行经济科技专场记者会" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-sciowhole102-http]; "深圳市发展和改革委员会副主任朱云" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-sciowhole102-http]; "第一个方面是打造全栈自主的基础算力底座" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-sciowhole102-http] (the first is to build a base of computing that is its own through the whole stack); "训练芯片方面，华为构建从芯片、集群、软件栈、开发框架到行业应用的全栈自主可控AI计算生态，全面对标替代英伟达计算生态，昇腾910系列芯片性能比肩国外主流产品，成为国产AI芯片的主力军" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-sciowhole102-http] (for training chips, Huawei has built an independently controllable AI computing ecosystem from chips and clusters to the software stack, the development framework and industry uses, benchmarked in every respect against Nvidia's computing ecosystem and substituting for it; the Ascend 910 chips match mainstream foreign products and have become the main force among home-made AI chips); and of the years to 2030, "奋力将深圳打造成为自主可控算力技术引领地" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-sciowhole102-http] (to strive to make Shenzhen a leading place for independently controllable computing technology). A city official's account of a company's work and of the city's aim; Zhu states no rule, no share and no instruction to buyers, and says nothing of Nvidia's chips in use in the city. Cuts: FOR (the declared line, at city level: a planning official names Nvidia's ecosystem as the thing to be replaced), CONTEXT (limit 1: a city's words, not the centre's).

### 2.6 Published commentary in state and Party media, and an industry body's call

(Tier 1 as published commentary. None of these is an order; they warn, urge or advise. A list of 75 items in central state and Party media on Nvidia chips, Jul 2025 to Nov 2026, is tables/state_media_items.tsv (rows 1 to 42 a census of Nov 23, 2026; rows 43 to 75 added from searches and links of Nov 24, 2026; of the 39 items dated after September 2025, 20 come from the census of Nov 23 and 19 from the searches of Nov 24, so for those months the table holds what two rounds of searches found, a sample of what these outlets printed and not a full count): in the 75 items found, 19 only warn about Nvidia's chips (two of the census's first seven do not speak of buying, and the three census rows added to them, 9, 10 and 11, carry the regulator's notice or demand and speak of the sales to China, not of buying), 16 of them dated Jul 31 to Sept 24, 2025 and three later, all in Global Times in 2026: an industry analyst's words in news reports of Mar 3 and Mar 18, and the paper's own unsigned commentary of Nov 6; 7 mix a warning with a welcome, four of them later; 5 welcome Nvidia or its sales in China (rows 6, 8, 36, 59 and 67 of the table; two of them are quoted in section 5); the rest are news (22), about self-reliance (16) or other (6). Say «in the 75 items found», never a universal. A baseline for January 2024 to June 2025, gathered later by other routes and not a census, is kept apart (tables/state_media_baseline_2024_2025h1.tsv): 49 items, 36 of them in outlets this list covers (the other 13 are in People's Daily group papers it never holds: Securities Times, People's Tribune, China City News); one only warns (a column credited to People's Posts and Telecommunications News, carried on the site of Outlook Weekly, Mar 13, 2025, against relying on the H20), none mixes a warning with a welcome, 4 welcome. With it, 20 items that only warn were found from January 2024 to November 2026: 16 dated Jul 31 to Sept 24, 2025, one before and three after.)

- **Dec 3, 2024 · Internet Society of China, statement on its own site** (an industry body's published call). It urges Chinese companies to be prudent in buying American chips, to widen cooperation with chip firms of other countries, and to use chips made in China by domestic and foreign-funded firms alike; its stated ground is US export controls, which it says have shaken trust in American chips. Words: "审慎选择采购美国芯片，寻求扩大与其他国家和地区芯片企业的合作，并积极使用内外资企业在华生产制造的芯片" [cn-against2-isc-supply-risk-20241203] (be prudent in choosing to buy American chips, seek wider cooperation with chip firms of other countries and regions, and actively use chips made in China by domestic and foreign-funded firms); "已经动摇业界对美国芯片产品的信任和信心" [cn-against2-isc-supply-risk-20241203]; "2024年12月03日" [cn-against2-isc-supply-risk-20241203]. It is a call for prudence, not abstention, and does not name Nvidia. Cuts: FOR, with that limit.
- **Aug 1, 2025 · People's Daily commentary account, signed commentary by Meng Fanzhe, as carried by Beijing Daily's client (labelled 人民日报评论)** (published commentary). After the cyberspace regulator's summons, it says that to keep cyberspace safe «sick» chips must never be put on duty. Words: "发展数字经济、建设数字中国，必须维护好网络空间安全，绝不能让“带病”的芯片上岗" [cn-acts-peoplesdaily-bjd-20250801] (to develop the digital economy ... cyberspace security must be kept; «sick» chips must never be allowed on duty); "人民日报评论:英伟达,让我怎么相信你?" [cn-acts-peoplesdaily-bjd-20250801]. Global Times in English the same evening says it appeared on the account's WeChat and Weibo with a video: "published on its social media accounts WeChat and Sina Weibo an article titled" [cn-le13-gt-pd-comment-en-202508]. Cuts: FOR.
- **Aug 4, 2025 · Economic Information Daily (Xinhua's paper), signed commentary by Wu Wei, on Xinhua's site** (published commentary). It warns that using foreign computing products with hidden security risks in government information systems and the industrial internet would have dire results, and calls developing independently controllable home-made computing a strategic question of national security. Words: "这些领域若使用存在安全隐患的境外算力产品，后果不堪设想。" [cn-media-xinhua-domestic-security]; "更是关乎国家安全的战略命题" [cn-media-xinhua-domestic-security]. Cuts: FOR.
- **Aug 4, 2025 · China Daily, unsigned commentary** (published commentary). It backs the summons. Words: "Nvidia needs to prove both its chips and intentions are clean." [cn-media-chinadaily-ascend-comment]. Cuts: FOR.
- **Aug 10, 2025 · Yuyuan Tantian, an account of the state broadcaster China Media Group, on its own Tencent News channel (posted 11:47) and on CCTV News's channel (11:08)** (published commentary). It calls the H20 neither green, nor advanced, nor safe, and says consumers can of course choose not to buy it. Words: "当一款芯片，既不环保，也不先进，更不安全时，作为消费者，我们当然可以选择，不买。" [cn-le13-yuyuan-qq-20250810b] (when a chip is neither green nor advanced, and still less safe, we as consumers can of course choose not to buy it); "无论从哪个角度讲，H20对于中国来说，都算不上是一款安全的芯片。" [cn-le13-yuyuan-qq-20250810b]; The Paper's copy, cited before: "作为消费者，我们当然可以选择，不买" [cn-media-yuyuan-thepaper]. On Apr 24, 2026 China Media Group's research centre listed the piece as entry 2 of its 6 entries for the China Journalism Award: "现对总台报送参评国际传播的6件作品进行公示，欢迎监督、评议。" [cn-le13-cctv-award-notice-20260424]; the list itself stands in the notice's data copy, where entry 2 is the piece, "玉渊谭天丨独家观察" [cn-ga02-cctv-award-notice-api-20260424]; it is not among the 378 winners of Nov 8, 2026 (芯片, 后门, 英伟达 and H20 each 0 times in the list; another of the 6 entries is found, so the list could show a hit) [cn-le13-cja36-winners-newscn-20261108]. The broadcaster put the piece forward as its own work eight months later; do not call it an award. Cuts: FOR.
- **Sept 10, 2025 · Zhonghua Dushubao (Guangming Daily group), signed commentary by Shi Aiwu, page 18** (published commentary; DISPUTED). It says the H20 was found to carry a hardware module disguised as a temperature-sensor driver sending data to Virginia every six hours, and presents this as from the regulator's notice; in the regulator's notice the strings 温度传感器, 弗吉尼亚 and 6小时 are each found 0 times (the control words are found) [cn-policy-cac-h20-original]. Words: "该芯片被发现内置一个伪装成“温度传感器驱动”的硬件模块，每6小时向美国弗吉尼亚州某IP地址发送加密数据包" [cn-media-gmw-book-backdoor]. Use only as an example of a claim the official text does not make. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Sept 24, 2025 · Science and Technology Daily, report of a security forum** (press report of a forum; the line is the reporter's summary, not a quotation of the academician). Words: "沈昌祥在论坛总结中强调，政企用户应加快国产软硬件替代进程" [cn-media-stdaily-security-forum] (Shen Changxiang stressed in his summing-up that government and enterprise users should speed up substitution with home-made software and hardware). Cuts: FOR, weakly.
- **Dec 11, 2025 · China Daily's site, signed commentary by Wang Jingyao credited to 21st Century Media** (published commentary; the saved copy holds the opening only). Words: "For China, the twists from bans to partial approvals only confirm that the path of self-reliant development in core technologies is the right one" [cn-le13-chinadaily-20251211]. Cuts: FOR on direction.
- **Dec 13, 2025 · Science and Technology Daily, signed commentary by Hu Dingkun** (published commentary). It concedes that the H200 may be a ready answer for some firms' computing, and says China must not be content with that. Words: "毋庸讳言，H200或可为中国部分企业的AI算力提供一种唾手可得的解决方案，但我们绝不能满足于此。" [cn-le13-stdaily-h200-chipact-20251212] (it must be said that the H200 may offer some Chinese firms a ready solution for AI computing, but we must never be content with that). Cuts: both: FOR (it grants the firms' need) and FOR on direction.
- **Dec 14, 2025 · Economic Daily, column 科创之声, signed by She Huimin, page 5, on its own site and e-paper** (published commentary). Chinese users may buy the H200 selectively but will not take it wholesale again; home-made AI chips are already on the official purchase list; firms still fear a new cut-off or review after buying. Words: "中国用户可能会选择性购买H200芯片，但不会再全盘埋单。" [cn-le13-ed-h200-cecn-20251214]; "当前，国产AI芯片已纳入官方采购清单。" [cn-le13-ed-h200-cecn-20251214] (home-made AI chips have now been included in the official purchase list); "中国企业依然担忧采购H200芯片后，再遭断供或审查。" [cn-le13-ed-h200-cecn-20251214]. The Eastmoney relay cited before reads 买单 for the original's 埋单 [cn-media-economic-h200-eastmoney]. Cuts: mixed: FOR on home-made first, AGAINST a reading of no purchases (it expects selective buying).
- **Jan 14, 2026 · Global Times editorial (English), also on the Shanghai government information office's site** (published commentary; the original is dated "Published: Jan 14, 2026 11:47 PM", the Shanghai copy Jan 15). Words: "As of press time, the Chinese side had not yet issued an official response to the US policy." [cn-ga02-gt-editorial-h200-conditional-202601] [cn-le13-shio-voices-20260115]. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Feb 6, 2026 · Study Times (published by the Central Party School), page 2, signed by Zhang Jin, researcher at the Xi Jinping Economic Thought research centre** (published commentary; about robots, not data centres). Dependence on foreign high-end AI chips must still fall; Nvidia's interface standards indirectly control the hardware path; the same article calls for deeper opening and cooperation. Words: "高端AI芯片、高精度传感器、核心算法等对外依存度仍需降低" [cn-le12-studytimes-embodied-20260206]; "英伟达形成基于芯片架构的硬件接口标准，全球多数具身智能机器人企业需适配该标准以保障兼容性，间接掌控硬件技术路径" [cn-le12-studytimes-embodied-20260206]; "深化高水平开放合作，主动融入全球创新网络" [cn-le12-studytimes-embodied-20260206]; "作者系习近平经济思想研究中心研究员" [cn-le12-studytimes-embodied-20260206]. Cuts: FOR on direction, weakly.
- **May 1, 2026 · Qiushi's economics desk and CCID, joint study group, the original on Qiushi's site** (published analysis; the copy carried by the data administration is in section 3). At one home-made chip firm in Shanghai, the group learned, customers care most whether the chip is compatible with CUDA, though its hardware's computing-power figures near the international mainstream. Words: "尽管其硬件算力指标已接近国际主流水平，但客户最关心的却是“能不能兼容CUDA”" [cn-media-qiushi-cuda-original]. Cuts: FOR (the companies' own reason to stay with Nvidia).
- **Oct 26, 2026 · Xinhua client, reposted on the state-assets regulator's site** (a published state-media report). Home-made clusters of the computing network are lighting up faster; central SOEs plan about 2 trillion yuan of investment this year across all six networks (a one-year figure for six networks, not Bloomberg's five-year figure for AI data centres: do not join them). Words: "算力网国产集群加快点亮" [cn-le27-sasac-sixnets-xinhua-20261026]; "围绕“六张网”建设，今年国资央企年度计划安排相关投资约2万亿元" [cn-le27-sasac-sixnets-xinhua-20261026]; "国产算力芯片、高端服务器、智算集群集成解决方案、高效散热系统、算力调度平台软件均迎来大规模采购需求" [cn-le27-sasac-sixnets-xinhua-20261026] (Xinhua's account, without quotation marks, of what a Tsinghua University researcher said). Cuts: FOR on direction.
- **Dec 24, 2025 · Xiang Ligang, analyst, to Global Times, as carried by The Paper** (a named person in state media). China welcomes a more open US stance, but the chips exported must be safe, green and fit China's AI needs, and policy must be kept stable. Words: "中关村信息消费联盟理事长项立刚23日告诉《环球时报》记者" [cn-le27-thepaper-gt-xiang-h200-20251224]; "中国乐见美国在高科技出口上采取更加开放与合作的立场，但要保证向中国出口的芯片安全、环保、符合中国AI发展的应用要求" [cn-le27-thepaper-gt-xiang-h200-20251224]. The view that buyers must apply to the government is only in a self-media post attributed to him (Tier 2, «Leads only» there). He had said nearly the same to Global Times on Dec 9, 2025, printed by Huanqiu on Dec 10, with 人工智能 for AI: "中国乐见美国在高科技出口上采取更加开放与合作的立场，但要保证向中国出口的芯片安全、环保、符合中国人工智能发展的应用要求" [cn-ga02-hqtime-policy-shift-h200]. Cuts: mixed.
- **Sept 24, 2026 · Party group of the State Administration for Market Regulation, institutional account on the Party members' paper's site, crediting 旗帜网** (published account of a state body). It files the Nvidia investigation under carrying out the Party centre's major decisions, daring to struggle, and issuing authoritative statements at important moments; it says nothing about buying. Words: "坚持从政治上认识和把握市场监管工作，敢于斗争、善于斗争，依法对英伟达、高通公司、杜邦中国集团立案调查，在重要时间节点上发布权威声音，有力维护国家利益。" [cn-media-party-samr-september] (keep to understanding and grasping market regulation from a political standpoint, dare to struggle and be good at it, open formal investigations into Nvidia, Qualcomm and DuPont China Group under law, issue authoritative statements at important moments, and firmly uphold the national interest). Do not use it to say Beijing wanted no sales. Cuts: FOR (the case as a political act), and CONTEXT for the fifth limit (what Beijing meant).
- **Oct 16, 2026 · Economic Daily, 时评, by Wang Chenwei and Liu Jianhan, page 5** (published commentary; no method given). Words: "DeepSeek V4今年在华为昇腾芯片首发，实现国产软硬件生态绑定，推理成本仅为GPT-4的1/70，硬件成本为英伟达方案的1/3。" [cn-le13-ed-efficiency-cecn-20261016]. Cuts: AGAINST (a cost reason of the firms' own).
- **Nov 6, 2026 · Global Times (English), Viewpoint, unsigned** (published commentary). On the Reuters report of about 600,000 H200 chips, which it says neither the Chinese nor the US government nor Nvidia has confirmed, it argues that Chinese companies weigh supply stability, roadmaps and the security of supply chains, and that a return to large-scale purchases would go against commercial logic; it does not speak of backdoors. Words: "To expect them to disregard these risks and resume large-scale purchases is simply contrary to the most basic principles of commercial logic." [cn-ga02-gt-isnt-buying-opinion-202611]; "Although neither the Chinese government nor the US government nor Nvidia has confirmed the report" [cn-ga02-gt-isnt-buying-opinion-202611]. Cuts: FOR (state media defends not buying), AGAINST a state order (it gives the companies' commercial reasons).
- **Nov 18, 2026 · Global Times (English), quoting Xiang Ligang, whom it calls a Chinese telecom industry expert** (a named person in state media). Words: "many inference tasks don't need high-end chips at all." [cn-media-gt-nov-chip-growth]. Cuts: AGAINST (a reason of the firms' own for home-made chips).
- **Sept 17, 2025 and Jan 12, 2026 · Cyber Security Association of China, industry self-discipline initiative on AI security; and the cybersecurity standards committee's practice guide on AI accelerator chips** (an industry body's published call, carried in full by 安全内参 because the association's own page refused the search tool; and the committee's own monthly report). The association's initiative, launched with more than 60 units including leading internet and AI firms, asks for technical exchange, open-source collaboration and complementary supply chains; the committee's guide sets security functions and test methods for AI accelerator chips as a reference for evaluation and certification. Neither names a maker or tells buyers to drop one (英伟达 0 in both; control: 芯片 4 in the committee's report). Words: "本次《倡议》由中国网络空间安全协会会同行业企业共同发起" [cn-ga31-mssposts-17]; "9月17日上午,在云南昆明召开的2025年国家网络安全宣传周人工智能安全治理分论坛上" [cn-ga31-mssposts-17]; "鼓励技术交流、开源协作、供应链互补等" [cn-ga31-mssposts-17]; "1 月 12 日,秘书处组织编制的《网络安全标准实践指南——人工智能加速芯片安全功能技术规范》发布" [cn-ga31-mssposts-18]; "也为开展相应的安全评估和检测认证活动提供参考" [cn-ga31-mssposts-18]. The association's group standard for data-centre AI accelerators (T/CSAC 024—2025, Nov 28, 2025) and its call of Oct 16, 2024 for a cybersecurity review of Intel's products sold in China were found by a search and not saved (section 11). Cuts: CONTEXT (a published security yardstick for AI chips, open to any maker).
- **Dec 3 to Dec 5, 2024 · Three more industry bodies beside the Internet Society of China, and the commerce ministry asked about them** (industry bodies' published statements, the semiconductor and automobile associations' as the People's Daily client compiles them from their WeChat accounts, carried on People's Daily Online, the communications enterprises association's on its own site; and the ministry's press-conference transcript). After the US rule of Dec 2, 2024 the China Semiconductor Industry Association said US chips were "no longer safe, no longer reliable" and Chinese industries "will have to" buy them cautiously; the automobile manufacturers' association advised carmakers to buy US chips cautiously; the China Association of Communication Enterprises said the same for telecoms, asked the government to investigate the supply-chain security of critical information infrastructure, and asked firms to treat chips made in China by domestic and foreign-funded firms equally. Asked by Reuters on Dec 5 how the ministry sees US chips and their safety, the ministry's spokesman He Yadong spoke of the uncertainty that US controls bring to trade; he did not call US chips unsafe. None of the four statements names Nvidia. Words: "谨慎采购美国芯片!中国互联网协会等四协会发声" [cn-ga31-early2024-12]; "美国芯片产品不再安全、不再可靠,中国相关行业将不得不谨慎采购美国芯片" [cn-ga31-early2024-12]; "协会建议中国汽车企业谨慎采购美国芯片" [cn-ga31-early2024-12]; "应谨慎采购美国芯片" [cn-acts-cace-20241203]; "呼吁政府开展关键信息基础设施供应链安全调查" [cn-acts-cace-20241203]; "平等对待内外资企业在华生产的产品" [cn-acts-cace-20241203]; "中国多家行业协会呼吁中企审慎采购美国芯片,并表示美国芯片"不再安全",请问中国商务部如何看待美国芯片产品?如何判断美国芯片的安全与否?" [cn-ga31-early2024-2] (Reuters's question); "对半导体领域正常贸易往来带来极大不确定性,对全球产业链供应链稳定构成了威胁" [cn-ga31-early2024-2] (the answer: US controls bring great uncertainty to normal trade in semiconductors and threaten the stability of global industrial and supply chains). Cuts: FOR (industry bodies close to the state urge caution in buying American chips, on the ground of US controls), with two limits: the made-in-China clause keeps foreign-funded makers in, and the ministry, asked, did not endorse "unsafe".
- **Nov 18 to Nov 24, 2026 · State media on home-made computing in the week after Nvidia said it was no longer shipping the H200 (section 3.2): People's Daily's report signed by the Xi Jinping Economic Thought research centre; a forecast of the China Center for Information Industry Development (CCID); the national supercomputing internet's count** (published texts in state media). The People's Daily report of Nov 24 (page 4) asks to "promote the adapted use of home-made computing with home-made models" and cites Guangdong's support for full-stack independently controllable AI computing, Huawei's Ascend chips "breaking through foreign technology blockades". Xinhua Daily Telegraph, on Xinhua's site, reports the forecast of CCID (中国电子信息产业发展研究院) at the opening ceremony of the World Computing Conference, which the page's photograph dates Nov 20: by 2030, 85% or more of home-made models adapted to home-made chips, a forecast, not a target. China Securities Journal (Nov 18) gives the national supercomputing internet platform, by September 2026, more than 250,000 GPU cards pooled, 95% of them home-made computing. None names Nvidia (英伟达 0 in each). Words: "习近平经济思想研究中心 《人民日报》( 2026年11月24日 第 04 版)" [cn-ga31-livecn-29]; "推动国产算力与国产模型适配使用" [cn-ga31-livecn-29]; "支持龙头企业构建全栈自主可控的人工智能计算生态,华为昇腾芯片突破国外技术封锁" [cn-ga31-livecn-29]; "预计到2030年,“国模”适配“国芯”比例将达85%以上" [cn-ga31-livecn-2]; "截至2026年9月,平台注册用户突破170万" [cn-ga31-livecn-23]; "汇聚超25万张GPU卡,其中95%为国产算力" [cn-ga31-livecn-23]. Cuts: FOR on direction (state media press home-made computing in that week); none of these texts names Nvidia.
- **Mar 13, 2025 · Outlook Weekly (瞭望周刊社, as the page's title names it), on its own site, signed commentary by Xu Yong, credited to People's Posts and Telecommunications News (人民邮电报)** (published commentary). It calls the H20 a cut-down H100 through which Nvidia draws Chinese firms into depending on it, and warns that over-reliance on the chip would leave China's AI industry on the back foot; it makes no security claim and does not tell buyers not to buy. Words: "英伟达借助H20芯片营造出一种“可用且实用”的假象，诱导国内企业对其产生依赖。" [cn-ga31-mediabase-4] (through the H20 Nvidia creates the illusion that it is "usable and practical", drawing domestic firms to depend on it); "对H20芯片的过度依赖，会使中国人工智能产业在全球竞争中陷入被动局面" [cn-ga31-mediabase-4] (over-reliance on the H20 would leave China's AI industry passive in global competition); "2025-03-13 11:08:34 来源： 人民邮电报" [cn-ga31-mediabase-4]. It is the only item that warns among those found for January 2024 to June 2025 (the baseline of tables/state_media_items.tsv, tables/state_media_baseline_2024_2025h1.tsv; not a census). Cuts: FOR, with that limit (against reliance, not purchase).
- **Dec 17, 2024 · Yuyuan Tantian (China Media Group's account: this section's entry of Aug 10, 2025), video on CCTV's news site, series 谭清楚** (published commentary; the saved page holds the caption only). Eight days after the antitrust case was announced it asks whether China really "cannot do without" Nvidia's chips. Words: "近期，英伟达涉嫌违反反垄断被市场监管总局立案调查。" [cn-ga31-mediabase-19]; "中国要发展人工智能“离不开”英伟达芯片，事实真的如此吗？" [cn-ga31-mediabase-19] (that China "cannot do without" Nvidia's chips to develop AI: is that really so?). A question about dependence, not advice against buying; the same account's piece of Aug 10, 2025 above is the first to say buyers can choose not to buy. Cuts: FOR on direction, weakly.
- **Dec 3 and 4, 2024 · CCTV's news site, carrying the China Semiconductor Industry Association and the China Association of Automobile Manufacturers (with the Internet Society of China, above)** (industry bodies' published calls, carried by the state broadcaster). After the US curbs of Dec 2, 2024 the semiconductor association calls US chips no longer safe or reliable and says the Chinese industries concerned will have to buy them cautiously; the car makers' body calls US car chips no longer reliable or safe and advises car makers to buy US chips cautiously. Words: "美国芯片产品不再安全、不再可靠，中国相关行业将不得不谨慎采购美国芯片" [cn-ga31-mediabase-44] (US chip products are no longer safe or reliable, and the Chinese industries concerned will have to buy US chips cautiously); "协会建议中国汽车企业谨慎采购美国芯片" [cn-ga31-mediabase-43]. Neither names Nvidia; the ground given is the unreliability of US supply under US controls. Cuts: FOR, with that limit (caution, not abstention; US chips in general).
- **Jan 23, 2024 · Yuyuan Tantian (China Media Group's account: this section's entry of Aug 10, 2025), on CCTV's news site** (published commentary). On Jensen Huang's week in China it relays US press reports that Chinese customers did not want Nvidia's downgraded chips and that many Chinese cloud firms cut their Nvidia chip orders and bought chips developed by Huawei and other home firms instead, and says many Chinese companies think cooperation with Nvidia has hit its ceiling; it blames US controls and gives no advice on buying. Words: "美媒提到，中国的不少云计算公司都减少了对英伟达的芯片订单，并转而购买华为等本土企业开发的芯片。" [cn-ga31-intb-yyt-20240123]; "因此不少中国公司都认为，与英伟达的合作已经摸到了天花板。" [cn-ga31-intb-yyt-20240123] (so many Chinese companies think cooperation with Nvidia has reached its ceiling). Cuts: CONTEXT (buyers' reluctance as state media described it, laid to US curbs).

## 3. Official and filed documents on what the companies used and wanted

- **Shanghai Municipal Commission of Economy and Informatization**, written reply to proposal No. 0093 of the city's political consultative conference, from its members Fang Qizhong and Zhu Minghua ("方奇钟、朱明华委员" [cn-counter-b11-5]), dated Apr 30, 2025 at its foot ("上海市经济和信息化委员会 2025年4月30日") and published on the commission's site on Nov 10, 2025 ("发布日期:2025-11-10"). (Note, Nov 23, 2026: for about two hours this line said the reply was undated. That was an error: the date stands in the saved page's body and not in its text extract, which was all that had been searched.): "互联网企业自建算力集群,满足市场化企业需求,主要以英伟达高性能卡为主;三大运营商主要购置华为等国产化智算集群" (internet companies build their own computing clusters to meet the needs of market-oriented enterprises, mainly with Nvidia high-performance cards; the three telecom operators mainly buy Huawei and other home-made intelligent-computing clusters) [cn-counter-b11-5]. The passage is about Shanghai's computing suppliers: "从算力供应商来看 , 主要集中在三大电信运营商、上海仪电，以及商汤、腾讯、 阿里、火山等互联网企业" [cn-counter-b11-5], and the operators' purchases are to "提升本市智算多样性" [cn-counter-b11-5] (raise the diversity of the city's intelligent computing). This is a Chinese government body describing, for its own city, the split the position rests on: internet companies mainly on Nvidia cards, the three big operators mainly buying home-made hardware. The operators' own national tenders point the same way (section 3.6).
- **Qiushi and CCID investigation**, carried by the National Data Administration, May 3, 2026: "数百万开发者深度绑定CUDA生态,迁移成本高昂、适配周期漫长,国产替代即便性能达标,规模化应用仍面临障碍" (millions of developers are deeply tied to the CUDA ecosystem; migration is costly and adaptation slow; even where home-made substitutes meet the performance mark, use at scale faces obstacles) [cn-policy-qiushi-cuda-gap].
- **A company's question to the commerce ministry**, on the ministry's public message board, left on Jul 30, 2026 ("留言时间: 2026-07-30") and answered on Aug 19, 2026 (corrected Nov 23, 2026: this file had given the reply's date as the question's; the reply, signed by the ministry's security and control bureau, speaks only of exporters, who must apply for a licence where an item is found to be controlled, "物项确定属于管制范围的，出口经营者应当向国务院商务主管部门申请许可", and does not address imports): "我司计划合规进口H200数据中心GPU,本次咨询该类高端算力芯片的进口合规政策与申报流程" (our company plans to import H200 data-centre GPUs in compliance, and asks about the import compliance policy and declaration procedure for such high-end computing chips) [cn-acts-mofcom-inquiry-20260819].
- **Zhipu, results announcement to the Hong Kong exchange**, Mar 31, 2026: "Faced with the computing power shortage that has outstripped supply since February 2026, we will continue to increase our investments, particularly in the “Day 0” adaptation of domestic chips" [cn-le29-zhipu-results-20260331-en] (a byte-identical copy from Zhipu's investor site, by a sha256 check).
- **Capitalonline (a listed Chinese cloud company), investor-meeting record filed with the exchange**, Feb 17, 2025, asked where it finds the gap between home-made chips and Nvidia's in use: "英伟达芯片通用性强,能在多种 AI 场景 稳定高效运行。国产芯片在通用性上面临一定挑战" (Nvidia chips are highly general-purpose and run stably and efficiently in many AI settings; home-made chips face some challenge in generality); "英伟达的芯片生产规模大,采购成本相对较低, 而国产芯片的生产规模较小,因此采购成本较高" (Nvidia's chips are made at large scale and cost less to buy, while home-made chips are made at smaller scale and cost more) [cn-policy-capitalonline-chip-use]. The company side of the conflict in a filed document; it mentions no state instruction.
- **Nvidia, Form 10-Q** (quarter to Jul 28, 2024), fifteen months before the reported purchase guidance: "an agency of the Chinese government announced an Action Plan that endorses new standards regarding the compute performance per watt and per memory bandwidth of accelerators used in new and renovated data centers in China."; "If the Chinese government modifies or implements the Action Plan in a way that effectively prevents us from being able to design products to meet the new standard, this may restrict the ability of customers to use some of our data center products" [cn-policy-nvidia-plan-2024]. Which agency and plan it means is not established: the plan is not among the saved pages.
- **Against a simple conflict: the companies' own chips.** Baidu, Form 20-F for 2024: "Baidu AI chips have been used for our search engine, cloud, and Xiaodu’s business needs, while powering our deep learning computing needs." [cn-policy-baidu-own-chip-wayback, an Internet Archive capture of May 27, 2025]. Alibaba, results release of Mar 19, 2026: "chip design subsidiary T-Head has brought proprietary GPUs into scaled production." [cn-policy-alibaba-own-gpu]. Two of the companies named as wanting Nvidia chips tell investors they have programmes of their own; neither says what share of its computing those chips carry. Baidu's report ties its chip-supply risk to US export controls, not to Chinese pressure: "如果我們因出口管制而無法採購足夠數量的先進半導體芯片，我們可能面臨重大挑戰，包括產能受限、成本上升及效能下降。" [cn-le07-baidu-2025-hkar].
- **Results calls (added Nov 23, 2026; quotations checked by script).** Nvidia, May 20, 2026: "While the US government has approved licenses for H200 to be shipped to China-based customers, we have yet to generate any revenue, and we are uncertain whether any imports will be allowed into the country." [cn-o38-nvda-q1fy27-call-transcript-pdf]. Nvidia, call for the quarter ended Oct 25, 2026: "Q3 sales of licensed Hopper Data Center products to China was $2.4 billion. Our guidance does not have any licensed Hopper Data Center products included, and we are no longer shipping licensed Hopper Data Center products to China." [cn-o38-nvda-q3fy27-call-transcript-pdf]; no reason is given. Tencent's chief strategy officer, May 13, 2026 (as a stock-data site's transcript and Sina Tech's Chinese record [cn-ga31-tencentcall-1] label the speaker; a Chinese transcript on Xueqiu labels the same answer the chief financial officer's [cn-ga31-tencentcall-25]; Tencent's replay was not reachable): "a combination of policy restrictions on certain, you know, foreign design GPUs being brought into China. The China design GPUs, you know, facing limited fab capacity within China." [cn-o36-tencent-call-20260513-sa]; he does not say whose policy. The same officer, Mar 18, 2026: "higher-end imported GPUs which are now becoming available again" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20260318-sa]. Across 39 saved calls of Alibaba, Tencent and Baidu (Nov 2023 to Nov 2026) a reading of them found no executive naming Nvidia or a Chinese order: `outputs/A11_records/idea36_results_calls/results_calls.md`; across 61 calls of 8 companies (Jan 2025 to Nov 2026) a separate search found nobody saying Nvidia or Huawei, with a control (section 3.5 and section 8). The same Tencent officer on May 13, 2026 went on: "we haven't faced those sort of artificial additional constraints on CPU or networking chips" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20260513-sa].
- **Alibaba, annual report to the SEC** (year to Mar 31, 2026): "In September 2025, the Cyberspace Administration of China requested Chinese technology companies to halt purchases of certain chips." [cn-clips-alibaba-20f]
- **Nvidia, Form 10-Q** (quarter to Jul 26, 2026): the US "granted licenses that would allow us to ship small amounts of H200 products to specific China-based customers, but such sales were restricted by the PRC government" [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07].
- **Nvidia's own revenue from customers headquartered in China and Hong Kong, by quarter (added Nov 23, 2026; each figure read in a filing's own table or, where marked, got by subtraction of two such figures, in millions of dollars).** Quarter ended Oct 27, 2024: 8,142. Jan 26, 2025: 7,818 (by subtraction: 25,048 for the fiscal year less 17,230 for its first nine months). Apr 27, 2025: 9,659. Jul 27, 2025: 3,985. Oct 26, 2025: 2,973. Jan 25, 2026: 3,060 (by subtraction: 19,677 for the fiscal year less 16,617 for its first nine months). Apr 26, 2026: 4,550. Jul 26, 2026: 7,880. Oct 25, 2026: 11,050, of total revenue of 115,844. Sources: the quarterly report signed Nov 19, 2025 [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10], the annual report for the year ended Jan 25, 2026 [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2026], the quarterly reports for the quarters ended Apr 26 and Jul 26, 2026 [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-04] [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07], and the Form 8-K of Nov 17, 2026 [ms-p3-nvda-8k-20261117-pdf]. So the line fell by about two thirds between spring and autumn 2025 and then rose to its highest value of the nine quarters in the quarter ended Oct 25, 2026. All nine are on the headquarters basis Nvidia adopted in its 10-Q of Nov 19, 2025, with earlier periods recast; the filings before that counted by billing location, as that 10-Q says ("Previously, revenue by geographic area was reported based on the billing location of our customers" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10]), and the one earlier filing read here printed a lower figure for its quarter (5,416 for the quarter ended Oct 27, 2024) [man-ms-nvda-2024q3-10q-58a58e]. **What it does not show:** chips entering China. Nvidia's note on the table: "Revenue by geographic region is designated based on the location of the headquarters of direct customers. The end customer and shipping location may be different from our direct customers' headquarters location." [ms-p3-nvda-8k-20261117-pdf] The line covers every product, not only AI chips, and the same filing lists equipment makers and distributors among direct customers, "such as add-in board manufacturers, or AIBs, distributors, original design manufacturers, or ODMs, original equipment manufacturers, or OEMs" [ms-p3-nvda-8k-20261117-pdf]. Of the latest quarter Nvidia said that "Q3 sales of licensed Hopper Data Center products to China was $2.4 billion" [cn-o38-nvda-q3fy27-call-transcript-pdf]. Not yet used in the argument.

### 3.1 Nvidia's own words on the Chinese government, filing by filing

(Filings to the SEC. Full table: tables/nvidia_wording_by_filing.tsv, 109 rows.)

| Filed | Period | What the filing says of the Chinese government | Saved id |
|---|---|---|---|
| May 2023 to Aug 2024 | quarters to Apr 2023 through Jul 2024 | "In addition to USG export controls, the Chinese government may also impose restrictions that impact our ability to sell our products." [ms-p3-nvda-10q-2023-04-pdf]; Aug 2024: "The Chinese government may also impose restrictions on the sale to certain customers of our products" [ms-p3-nvda-10q-2024-07-pdf] | as named |
| Nov 20, 2024 | quarter to Oct 27, 2024 | first "encourage": "The Chinese government may also encourage customers to purchase from our China-based" [man-ms-nvda-2024q3-10q-58a58e] | man-ms-nvda-2024q3-10q-58a58e |
| Feb and May 2025 | year to Jan 26, 2025; quarter to Apr 27, 2025 | "The Chinese government may also encourage customers to purchase from our China-based competitors, or impose restrictions on the sale to certain customers of our products" [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2025] | man-ms-sec-10k-fy2025; man-ms-sec-10q-2025-04 |
| Aug 27, 2025 | quarter to Jul 27, 2025 | "The Chinese government has and may continue to encourage customers to purchase from our" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-07]; and, as Nvidia's example of how American draft bills hurt it: "For example, following U.S. legislative proposals calling for mandatory features in our chips, China's government publicly questioned whether our H20 products have built-in vulnerabilities" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-07], "discouraging customers from purchasing our products" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-07] | man-ms-sec-10q-2025-07 |
| Nov 19, 2025 | quarter to Oct 26, 2025 | "government has encouraged customers to purchase from our China-based competitors and restricted customers from" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10] "purchasing, importing, or using our data center products, including any China-specific product designed to comply with" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10] U.S. export controls | man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10 |
| Feb 2026 onward | year to Jan 25, 2026; quarters to Apr 26 and Jul 26, 2026 | the same sentence with "discouraged" for "restricted": "government has encouraged customers to purchase from our China-based competitors and discouraged customers" [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2026] | man-ms-sec-10k-fy2026; man-ms-sec-10q-2026-04; man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07 |

- **The two approvals, and the foreclosure put on US controls (Nov 19, 2025 onward).** From the 10-Q for the quarter to Oct 26, 2025 Nvidia says it cannot make a competitive product for China's data-centre market that both governments approve (its 10-Q for the quarter to Jul 27, 2025, signed Aug 27, 2025, named only the USG), and in the same filings it says it is already effectively foreclosed from the China market by US export controls. Words: "Under the current rules and geopolitical landscape, we are unable to create and deliver a competitive product for China's data center market that receives approval from both the USG and the Chinese government." [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10]; the 10-Q for the quarter to Jul 27, 2025, before: "We may be unable to create a competitive product for China's data center market that receives approval from the USG." [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-07]; "Although we are already effectively foreclosed from the China market by U.S. export controls" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10]; the annual report: "As of the end of fiscal year 2026, we were effectively foreclosed from competing in China's data center computing/compute market" [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2026]. Cuts: FOR (Beijing named as a second approver) and AGAINST (the foreclosure is put down to US controls) at once.
- **The H20 and H200 licences: «restricted by the PRC government» first in the 10-Q of Aug 26, 2026.** Earlier filings give amounts and no cause; the 10-Q for the quarter to Jul 26, 2026 says both chips' licensed sales were restricted by the PRC government, and the H200 charge follows that sentence directly. Words: Nov 2025, "but to date, we have generated approximately $50 million in H20 revenue under those licenses" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10]; Feb 2026, "We generated approximately $60 million in H20 revenue under those licenses." [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2026] and "To date, we have not generated any revenue under the H200 licensing program, and do not yet know whether any imports will be allowed into China." [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2026]; Aug 2026, "Beginning in August 2025, the USG granted licenses that would have allowed us to ship certain H20 products to certain China-based customers, but such sales were restricted by the PRC government, and we were unable to sell our H20 inventory." [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07]; "The U.S. government, or USG, granted licenses that would allow us to ship small amounts of H200 products to specific China-based customers, but such sales were restricted by the PRC government, and we have been unable to sell all the products for which we have licenses." [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07]; next: "During the first half of fiscal year 2027, we incurred a $0.4 billion charge associated with H200 for excess inventory and purchase obligations, as the demand for H200 products diminished." [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07]; and "After incurring that charge, we have made a fraction of the allowed shipments under the USG's H200 licensing program. Those shipments account for less than 1% of Data Center revenue in our most recent quarter." [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07]. Revenue under a licence is a sale to a China-based customer; it does not say where the chips went. Cuts: FOR.
- **American conditions on the H200, in the same filings.** The licences require inspection in the United States, a 25% tariff applies on import into the United States for that inspection, and Nvidia could not pass any of it on. Words: "The license requires that the H200s go through an inspection process in the United States prior to any shipment to the customer." [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2026]; "any H200 shipped under the new licensing program will be subject to a 25% tariff upon importation into the United States." [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2026]; "We have been unable to pass along any of the tariff to our customers, and do not anticipate doing so in the event we are able to sell licensed products into the China market." [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07]; and of the H20: "USG officials have expressed an expectation that the USG will receive 15% of the revenue generated from licensed H20 sales, but to date, the USG has not published a regulation codifying such requirement." [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-07]. Cuts: AGAINST «only Beijing stood in the H200's way».
- **The H20 under the April 2025 US licence rule.** Words: "Sales of our H20 products were $4.6 billion for the first quarter of fiscal year 2026 prior to the new export licensing requirements." [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-04]; "As a result of these new requirements, we incurred a $4.5 billion charge in the first quarter of fiscal year 2026 associated with H20 excess inventory and purchase obligations as the demand for H20 products diminished." [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-04]; the commentary on that quarter: "This outlook reflects a loss in H20 revenue of approximately $8.0 billion due to the recent export control limitations." [cn-le05-q1fy26-cfo-commentary]. Cuts: CONTEXT (limit 4: American curbs).
- **The antitrust finding, in Nvidia's words.** From Nov 2025 the filings say China's antitrust regulators found on Sept 15, 2025 that Nvidia's compliance with US export controls, which made it offer degraded products to China, discriminated unfairly against Chinese customers. Regulators had "inquired" from the 10-Q of Nov 2023. Words: "On September 15, 2025, China's antitrust regulators published their preliminary finding that our compliance with applicable U.S. export controls, which required us to offer degraded products to the Chinese market, discriminated unfairly against customers in the China market and therefore violated the terms" [man-ms-sec-10q-2025-10]; "Regulators in China have inquired about our sales and our efforts to supply the China market" [ms-p3-nvda-10q-2023-10-pdf]; Feb 2025: "For example, regulators in China are investigating whether complying with applicable U.S. export controls discriminates unfairly against customers in the China market." [man-ms-sec-10k-fy2025]. By Nvidia's account the case faults it for discriminating unfairly against customers in China by offering them degraded products. Cuts: AGAINST reading the case as Beijing wanting no sales; CONTEXT for the fifth limit.
- **Results calls on the same days speak of "geopolitical" issues and of the US export ban, and do not name Beijing as the cause.** Words: Aug 27, 2025, "We have not included H20 in our Q3 outlook as we continue to work through geopolitical issues." [ms-sgp-nvda-q2fy26-call-transcript-pdf]; Nov 19, 2025, "Sizable purchase orders never materialized in the quarter due to geopolitical issues and the increasingly competitive market in China." [ms-geo-nvda-q3fy26-call-transcript-pdf]; Aug 26, 2026, "And given ongoing geopolitical uncertainty, there is no China data center compute revenue in our forward outlook." [cn-o38-nvda-q2fy27-call-transcript-pdf]; and "In Q2, we shipped less than 1% of our total data center revenue in Hopper 200 products to customers based in China in accordance with the US government licenses." [cn-o38-nvda-q2fy27-call-transcript-pdf]; May 28, 2025, the chief executive: "Today, however, the $50 billion China market is effectively closed to US industry. The H20 export ban ended our" [ms-sgp-nvda-q1fy26-call-transcript-pdf] Hopper data-centre business in China. Every results release and commentary from Feb 25 to Nov 17, 2026 says "We are not assuming any Data Center compute revenue from China in our outlook." [cn-le05-q4fy26-cfo-commentary]. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **The 10-Q for the quarter ended Oct 25, 2026 had not been filed** when last looked for on Nov 25, 2026 (SEC record fetched 10:45 UTC; its newest entries are of Nov 23, 2026, and no 10-Q or NT 10-Q for that quarter is listed; the same record lists all eight earlier 10-Qs) [cn-ga31-official-sec-submissions-20261125-b]; it had not been filed on Nov 23 either [cn-le05-sec-submissions-20261123]. Words: "We plan to file our 10-Q in the coming weeks." [cn-counter-b17-1]. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Nvidia's statement on its H200 licences, as two news sites carried it in their reports of the released licence table** (a company statement relayed; neither page says on which day it was made). The Standard of Hong Kong, page stamped Nov 20, 2026, 04:57 Hong Kong time: "Nvidia said in a statement that the approvals were "subject to strict conditions and limited volumes." It added that "despite receiving some licenses, we have since stopped shipping H200s to China."" [cn-le04-standard-900000-20261120]. The Daily Caller, page stamped Nov 20, 2026, 20:29 UTC, quoting an Nvidia spokesperson at more length (the page says the spokesperson spoke to the Daily Caller News Foundation): "The review and licensing process spanned more than half a year, with approvals subject to strict conditions and limited volumes. Despite receiving some licenses, we have already stopped shipping H200s to China." [cn-le04-dailycaller-20261120]. A third saved page (24/7 Wall St, Nov 20, 2026) gives the same in its own words. No reason is given for the stop. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Feb 4, 2026 · Nvidia's spokesperson on US licence conditions, as quoted by Reuters** (a company statement relayed). Words: "We aren't able to accept or reject license conditions on our own" [cn-le10-bytedance-terms-20260204-standard]; "Although KYC is important, KYC is not the issue. For American industry to make any sales, the conditions need to be commercially practical, else the market will continue to move to foreign alternatives" [cn-le10-bytedance-terms-20260204-standard]. Cuts: AGAINST «only Beijing stood in the H200's way».

### 3.2 Nvidia's chief executive and its then interim chief executive, on the record (most through press reports)

- **May 21, 2025 · Jensen Huang at Computex, as paraphrased by Reuters.** Words: "Huang said Nvidia's market share in China dropped to 50%, from 95% at the start of former U.S. President Joe Biden's administration." [cn-usside-028]; "U.S. export controls on artificial intelligence to China were a failure" [cn-usside-028]. Cuts: AGAINST (Washington blamed).
- **Oct 6, 2025 · Jensen Huang at a Citadel Securities event, as reported by Fortune.** The date is from Tom's Hardware, "a live interview at Citadel Securities' Future of Global Markets 2025 event in New York on October 6" [cn-le05-huang-citadel-tomshardware]; Fortune, on Oct 19, 2025, has "In an interview with Citadel Securities on Tuesday" [cn-le05-huang-citadel-fortune]. Words: "We went from 95% market share to 0%, and so I can't imagine any policymaker thinking that that's a good idea, that whatever policy we implemented caused America to lose one of the largest markets in the world" [cn-le05-huang-citadel-fortune]. The policy he means is Washington's. Cuts: AGAINST.
- **Nov 5, 2025 · Reuters, in its story on a Financial Times report of that day, on what Jensen Huang had said before** (Reuters's own paraphrase of earlier remarks, one of which it dates to October; it does not give them as part of what the Financial Times reported). Words: "Chinese government has shut it out of its market." [cn-clips-huang-ai-race-reuters]; "Nvidia has not applied for U.S. export licenses to sell the chips in China, citing Beijing's stance toward the company" [cn-clips-huang-ai-race-reuters]. Cuts: FOR, weakly.
- **Jul 16, 2025 · Jensen Huang at the supply-chain expo, in the transcript of a media meeting as 21 Finance carried it on Jul 17 (edited by Tencent Technology; compiled from 21st Century Business Herald and Tencent Technology)** (a named person). Asked whether Nvidia had taken H20 orders: not yet, because the licences had not been approved; once approved it could take orders, and customers' needs may have changed since earlier orders were cancelled. Words: "目前还没有，因为相关许可尚未获批。" [cn-cnpress-21-huang-orders-20250717]; "一旦获得批准，我们就能正式接收订单了" [cn-cnpress-21-huang-orders-20250717]; "首先是客户的新订单时间点，毕竟之前的订单被取消了，他们的需求可能已经发生变化" [cn-cnpress-21-huang-orders-20250717]. The licences meant are Washington's. Cuts: CONTEXT (limit 4).
- **Jan 29, 2026 · Jensen Huang, on arriving in Taipei, as Bloomberg reported (relay), Voice of America's Chinese service rendered and the South China Morning Post quoted him.** Words: "It's up to the Chinese government now but they are still deciding, and we are waiting patiently." [cn-against2-huang-no-new-orders-20260129]; the same relay: "no new orders for the H200 chips were placed" [cn-against2-huang-no-new-orders-20260129] (Bloomberg's words); Voice of America, in direct speech: "我认为中国政府仍在决策中，我们尚未收到采购订单，我们也没有收到新闻报道所说的相关信息" [cn-ga31-counter-40] (I think the Chinese government is still deciding; we have not yet received purchase orders, and we have not received the information the news reports describe); the Post's English, read in an Internet Archive capture of Aug 19, 2026: "I believe the Chinese government is still deciding, we have not received purchase orders" [cn-ga31-inte-scmp-h200-approvals-20260128-wayback]. Cuts: FOR (Beijing as the gate at that date); AGAINST the reported approvals of Jan 28 as final, and against reading the "more than 2 million" ordered that Reuters reported on Dec 31, 2025 as purchase orders placed with Nvidia.
- **Mar 17, 2026 · Jensen Huang at a press conference, by Reuters and AFP.** He said the supply chain was getting fired up and that Nvidia had received purchase orders from many customers; Reuters's paraphrase names only US licences. Words: "Our supply chain is getting fired up" [cn-le10-huang-restart-20260317-yahoo]; "licenses to export the H200 from the U.S. government and has taken orders, Huang said." [cn-le10-huang-restart-20260317-kfgo]; AFP: "We have received purchase orders from many customers, and we're in the process of restarting our manufacturing" [cn-le10-huang-restart-20260318-fmt-afp]. Senator Coons's letter (section 4) gives a different account of the same remarks (approvals from both governments). Cuts: AGAINST (limit 3: some approved), FOR on demand.
- **Nov 17, 2026 · Colette Kress, then interim chief executive, on CNBC (CNBC's transcript, which it calls unofficial).** The stop is not China-only. Words: "And we are no longer shipping H200 at all, let alone not shipping it to China." [cn-against2-kress-global-h200-halt-20261117]; "Our shipments to China are approximately about 10 percent. But that is our gaming business." [cn-against2-kress-global-h200-halt-20261117]. Cuts: AGAINST reading the stop as a Chinese refusal.
- **Nov 22, 2026 · Nvidia's Board reinstated the chief executive** (Nvidia's Form 8-K, signed Nov 23, 2026; cited for this one sentence, which dates the word then in this section's heading and in the line above). Words: "On November 22, 2026, the Board of Directors of NVIDIA Corporation reinstated Jen-Hsun Huang as NVIDIA's President, Chief Executive Officer and member of the Board of Directors, effective immediately." [cn-ga06-nvda-8k-20261123-pdf]. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Jan 29, 2026 · Jensen Huang at Taipei airport, as Voice of America's Chinese service renders him: no purchase orders yet** (a press report of a named person's words, in Chinese). Words: "我认为中国政府仍在决策中，我们尚未收到采购订单" [cn-ga31-counter-40] (I think the Chinese government is still deciding; we have not yet received purchase orders); VOA sets this against Reuters's report of approvals for more than 400,000 H200s. The same day as the Bloomberg relay above. Cuts: CONTEXT (on that day Nvidia had no orders by his account; the reported approvals and orders of December and January rest on unnamed people).
- **Nov 7, 2025; Jan 29 and Mar 17, 2026 · Jensen Huang on Beijing's decision, in his own quoted words and in CNBC's and a Chinese outlet's accounts** (a named person on the record, through press reports and a press transcript in translation; no recording checked, and no Nvidia transcript found by a search of Nov 25, 2026). Nov 7, 2025, from Tainan (Bloomberg, on The Business Times): "It’s up to China when they would like Nvidia products to go back to serve the Chinese market" [cn-ga31-uswords-11]; "I look forward to them changing their policy, and hopefully we’ll be able to serve the Chinese market again." [cn-ga31-uswords-11]; and "There are no active discussions. Currently, we’re not planning to ship anything to China" [cn-ga31-uswords-11]. These are his words two days after the paraphrase of Nov 5 in this section. Jan 29, 2026, at Taipei's Songshan airport (Reuters, on Business Recorder): "And I’m hoping that also the Chinese government would allow Nvidia to sell the H-200, so they have to decide." [cn-ga31-uswords-10]; "the customers would very much like to have H200" [cn-ga31-uswords-10]; "And so we just have to wait patiently" [cn-ga31-uswords-10]. Bloomberg's quotation of the same day (this section's Jan 29 row) gives other sentences; neither outlet prints a transcript, so which words were said in what order is not settled. Mar 17, 2026: CNBC's own account (its LinkedIn post of that day): "Huang told CNBC that the company now has clearance from both sides." [cn-ga31-uswords-26], CNBC's paraphrase, which matches Senator Coons's letter (section 4) and not Reuters's (US licences only); Zhidx's Chinese rendering of the press Q&A, question 14: "我们已经获得了向中国众多客户销售H200的许可证，也已经收到了来自众多客户的采购订单，目前正处于恢复生产的过程之中" [cn-ga31-uswords-6] (we have obtained licences to sell the H200 to many customers in China, have received purchase orders from many customers, and are restarting production), naming no issuing government. Cuts: FOR (Beijing as the gate, in his words, Nov 2025 and Jan 2026); limit 3 (Mar 2026, an approval from «both sides» in CNBC's paraphrase only).

### 3.3 Other chip companies' filings (SEC; Tier 1 filings)

- **Nov 2024 to Nov 2026 · Qualcomm, 10-Ks for fiscal 2024, 2025 and 2026** (chips its customers put in their own devices; the passage does not speak of AI chips). Among the reasons some Chinese customers moved to their own or competitors' chips it lists pressure, encouragement, incentives or policies of the Chinese government, beside fear of losing access through US or Chinese government action. Words: "Due to various factors, including pressure, encouragement or incentives from, or policies of, the Chinese government (which has prioritized semiconductor self-sufficiency)" [cn-le06-qcom-10k-fy2025]; fiscal 2024: "Due to various factors, including pressure, encouragement or incentives from, or policies of, the Chinese government (such as its Made in China 2025 campaign)" [cn-le06-qcom-10k-fy2024]. Cuts: FOR (a second US chip maker naming Chinese pressure), with the limits stated.
- **Nov 2025 to Nov 2026 · AMD, 10-Qs and 10-K.** MI308 sales into China depend on customer demand, China's import control rules and licences; MI308 shipments began in the fourth quarter of 2025; in May and Aug 2026 AMD did not yet know whether China would allow MI325 imports, a sentence absent from its 10-Q of Nov 4, 2026 (0 places; «MI325» found 6 times); the MI325 licences carry a US inspection and a 25% tariff. Words: "Sales of our MI308 products into China depend on customer demand, China’s import control rules and our ability to obtain licenses." [cn-le06-amd-10q-2025-09]; "During the fourth quarter of fiscal year 2025, we began shipping products and reversed approximately $360 million of the charges recorded earlier in the year." [cn-le06-amd-10k-2025]; "We do not yet know whether any imports of MI325 products will be allowed into China." [cn-le06-amd-10q-2026-06]; "any MI325 shipped under the licenses will be subject to a 25% tariff upon importation into the United States for the inspection" [cn-le06-amd-10q-2026-03]; and on two Chinese decrees of 2026: "which establish or expand the legal authorities and mechanisms available to Chinese powers to investigate, restrict, and impose countermeasures on foreign organizations or persons" [cn-le06-amd-10q-2026-09]. Cuts: FOR (China's import rules named), AGAINST a no-entry reading (MI308 shipped), and AGAINST «only Beijing» (US conditions).
- **Oct 2024 to Oct 2026 · Micron, 10-Ks** (the published comparator, section 2.4). The regulator's 2023 decision bars critical information infrastructure operators from buying Micron products; the 2024 and 2025 reports add that the decision hurt its data-centre and networking business in China, a sentence the 2026 report drops (0 places; the control 4). Revenue from customers headquartered in mainland China, in $ millions: fiscal 2023 2,181; 2024 3,045; 2025 2,639; 2026 8,338. Words: "the CAC determined that critical information infrastructure operators in China may not purchase Micron products, impacting our revenue with companies headquartered in mainland China and Hong Kong" [cn-le06-mu-10k-fy2025]; "The CAC decision has impacted our business, particularly in the domestic data center and networking markets in China, and we have been working to mitigate that impact." [cn-le06-mu-10k-fy2025]; "Mainland China (excluding Hong Kong) 2,639 3,045 2,181" [cn-le06-mu-10k-fy2025]; "Mainland China (excluding Hong Kong) 8,338 2,639 3,045" [cn-le06-mu-10k-fy2026]. The filings do not tie the revenue figures to the decision. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Mar 2025 to Aug 2026 · Marvell, 10-Ks and 10-Q.** It cites the unreliable-supplier law and the Micron ban, says it is not now the subject of one of the antitrust probes China has announced against US technology companies, and expects no material effect. Words: "In May 2023, the Cyberspace Administration of China banned the sale of Micron Technology, Inc.’s products to certain entities in China" [cn-le06-mrvl-10k-fy2026]; "we do not expect these announced restrictions to materially impact us" [cn-le06-mrvl-10k-fy2026]. Cuts: AGAINST a general «Beijing shut out US chips».
- **May and Nov 2026 · Microchip, 10-K and 10-Q.** Words: "The Chinese government may restrict us from participating in the China market, or may prevent us from competing effectively with Chinese companies." [cn-le06-mchp-10k-fy2026]. (Its account of the anti-dumping probe is in section 2.4; its two filings date the Micron restriction differently, so quote them only as Microchip's words.) Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Jul 10, 2026 · SK hynix, final prospectus (an underwriter's print of the SEC copy).** The US-led «Chip 4» alliance led China to develop its own chip making, which has cut the percentage of SK hynix's sales made in China in recent years; and China's market regulator conditions its NAND prices in China. Words: "Such an alliance has led China to take measures to more actively develop its semiconductor manufacturing capabilities, which in turn has reduced the percentage of our sales in China in recent years." [cn-le06-skhynix-424b4-stifel]; "we expect the obligation to maintain a reasonable pricing policy will limit our ability to significantly increase the price of our NAND flash memory products sold in China in 2026" [cn-le06-skhynix-424b4-stifel]. Cuts: CONTEXT (direction FOR; the pricing condition AGAINST a one-way reading).
- **2025 to 2026 · Intel, Arista, Lumentum, Super Micro, TSMC.** Intel: "China responded with its own limitations on U.S. access to minerals and on companies supplying Taiwan's military" [cn-le06-intc-10k-2025]; Arista: "China has announced controls on both the use of Micron products and export controls on certain materials" [cn-le06-anet-10q-2026-09]; Lumentum: "the imposition of export control measures on customers in China may cause those customers to seek domestic alternatives to our products" [cn-le06-lite-10k-fy2026]; TSMC: "our current results of operations have not been materially affected by the expanded export control regulations or the novel rules or measures adopted to counteract them" [cn-le06-tsmc-20f-2025]. None states a Chinese restriction on buying its own products (bounded negatives in section 8). Cuts: CONTEXT.

### 3.4 Chinese companies' filings and announcements

- **Annual reports for 2025 of 15 US-listed Chinese companies: only Alibaba's says a Chinese authority asked Chinese technology companies to stop buying certain chips** (filings). Alibaba's sentence (its annual report, section 3 above) sits in a risk factor about American measures, whose next paragraph speaks of "These restrictions or sanctions, and similar or more expansive restrictions or sanctions that may be imposed by the United States or other jurisdictions in the future" [cn-clips-alibaba-20f]; the same report says "We and our technology partners are forced to develop equivalent technologies or components, or obtain equivalent technologies or components from sources outside the United States." [cn-clips-alibaba-20f] and that T-Head, its chip-design subsidiary with its own GPU in production at scale, is a business that "now contributes meaningfully to our cloud infrastructure supply" [cn-clips-alibaba-20f]. The other 14 tie chip-supply risk to US export controls; Baidu's Hong Kong annual report: "如果我們因出口管制而無法採購足夠數量的先進半導體芯片，我們可能面臨重大挑戰，包括產能受限、成本上升及效能下降。" [cn-le07-baidu-2025-hkar] (if export controls stop us buying enough advanced chips we may face major challenges, including limited capacity, higher costs and lower performance); GDS: "如果中國的公司（包括我們在中國的客戶）無法獲得具有可比經營業績及成本效益的相關限制項目（包括先進計算軟件及硬件）的替代源，中國人工智能技術的開發和採納可能會受到負面影響。" [cn-le07-gds-2025-hkar]. Cuts: FOR (Alibaba), and CONTEXT/AGAINST (14 name only US controls).
- **Nov 19, 2026 · Alibaba, results for the September quarter** (a filing). Capital spending rose partly on higher chip-component prices; a note on later events says, in the Chinese version, that the US remote-access rule of Nov 16, 2026 may affect its ability to get computing from certain providers outside China; the English version of the same filed announcement has «will impact» where the Chinese has «may affect», "The Rule, as written, will impact our company's ability to access compute from certain providers outside China." [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own25-alibabagroup] (its own entry in this section, dated Nov 19, 2026). Words: "該規則（按現有條文）可能會影響本公司從某些中國以外提供商獲取計算資源的能力。" [cn-le07-baba-2026q3-results] (the rule, as now written, may affect the company's ability to obtain computing resources from certain providers outside China); "we unveiled the Zhenwu V900, our next-generation AI chip for training and inference, with commercial release scheduled for the first quarter of 2027." [cn-le07-baba-2026q3-results-en]. Cuts: CONTEXT (the barrier named is American).

- **2025 to 2026 · Chinese chip makers' listing papers and Hygon's half-year report** (filings). Enflame's final prospectus (signed Sept 8, 2026) gives IDC's count of about 4 million AI cards shipped in China in 2025, about 2.2 million of them Nvidia's (about 55%), and elsewhere says international makers led by Nvidia held close to 60%; it says cloud firms are increasingly open to local makers, even a must, and that its sales to Tencent Technology (Shenzhen), direct and, in what it calls the AVAP model, through the server makers that customer designates, were 83.79% of its 2025 revenue. Its inquiry reply goes on from the passage the argument quotes: after the state's computing-hub project began at the end of 2022, computing-centre projects became one of the main early sources of demand for home-made AI chips. MetaX's «geopolitics» follows a list of US export-control rules. Hygon says localisation is turning from policy-driven to market-driven. Moore Threads says Nvidia's ecosystem is hard to surpass. Words: "2025年中国AI加速卡整体出货规模约400万张,其中英伟达以约220万张出货量占据约55%的市场份额" [cn-le08-enflame-final-prospectus]; "国内尤其是云厂商对于本土AI加速卡厂商合作态度日益开放,甚至是必选项" [cn-le08-enflame-final-prospectus]; "同期营业收入占比分别为33.34%、37.77%和83.79%" [cn-le08-enflame-final-prospectus]; "2022年末,国家“东数西算”国家算力枢纽节点建设工程启动,智算中心项目随之成为国产AI芯片初期主要的需求场景之一" [cn-filings-enflame-2026-inquiry]; the argument's lines, "出于商业化考量，互联网行业客户采购主要以国际产品为主" [cn-filings-enflame-2026-inquiry] and "报告期期初,国内 AI 算法、硬件及应用尚处于起步阶段" [cn-filings-enflame-2026-inquiry] (at the start of the reporting period, not «the first years»); MetaX: "鉴于英伟达目前在中国AI芯片市场的份额高达70%左右,出口管制政策将为中国AI芯片市场创造较大的供给缺口,下游智算中心的采购需求有望转移至国产厂商" [cn-filings-metax-2025-prospectus]; "国产GPU芯片渗透率低,面临技术标准适配及用户习惯迁移障碍" [cn-filings-metax-2025-prospectus]; Hygon: "国产化发展由政策驱动全面转向市场驱动的关键阶段" [cn-le08-hygon-2026-interim]; Moore Threads: "目前,英伟达的生态系统不易被超越" [cn-le08-moore-final-prospectus-cninfo]; "美国对华高端GPU出口限制促使中国企业加速国产替代进程" [cn-le08-moore-final-prospectus-cninfo]. (An earlier copy of that prospectus, saved from the Shanghai exchange's site, is an anti-bot page and is not cited.)
