- **Sept to Nov 2026 · How listed computing firms say they get GPU servers, and what holds supply back** (filings and exchange-platform answers; the firms' words, not the state's). Capitalonline's counsel, King & Wood (北京市金杜律师事务所), in its second supplemental legal opinion for the firm's share issue (filed on cninfo Oct 21, 2026): the firm buys foreign-sourced GPU servers through domestic suppliers who handle buying, import and delivery, "公司通常不直接向境外厂商采购相关设备，而是通过境内供应商采购高性能 GPU 服务器及相关配套设备，由相关供应商负责设备采购、进口及交付等环节" [cn-ga31-companies-72]; and, up to the opinion's date, "公司未发生因出口管制、贸易限制或其他贸易政策变化导致设备无法采购、客户无法交付或业务无法开展的情形" [cn-ga31-companies-72] (export controls, trade restrictions or other changes in trade policy never left the firm unable to buy equipment, deliver to customers or carry on business); on what limits the supply of such equipment the export controls it analyses are American, "以下重点分析美国针对高性能算力设备实施的出口管制规则及其影响" [cn-ga31-companies-72]; of China's own rules it finds the firm outside the export-control catalogues and the planned purchase no breach of Chinese prohibitions. SIE (协创数据, 300857), record of its investor calls of Nov 2 and 3, 2026 (no. 2026-005): building computing abroad is "受相关部门审批节奏影响" [cn-ga31-companies-82] (subject to the pace of approvals by the departments concerned, not named), and home-made supply waits on memory, "大量国产 GPU 因缺 HBM 目前在封装厂等待" [cn-ga31-companies-82] (many home-made GPUs are waiting at packaging plants for lack of HBM); on Nov 18, on the exchange's question platform as StockStar carries it, asked what it would do by an investor who said Nvidia had stated in its third-quarter results that it no longer supplies Hopper-series GPUs to China: "前期已通过合规渠道完成相关算力设备储备" [cn-ga31-companies-77] (it had already stocked the computing equipment through compliant channels); and, in answer to a separate question, "现阶段暂无通过东南亚算力设备向国内提供算力服务的安排" [cn-ga31-companies-77]. Tianyang's contract notice also provides that if the original maker leaves the seller unable to supply, the contract's purpose is deemed lost and, once the contract is ended, the seller refunds what was paid (its words are in this section's row on the contract terms of the three buyers of cards named PRO 5500; a second saved copy of the notice, cn-ga31-companies-7, has the same sha256). Cuts: AGAINST (foreign GPU servers bought through domestic suppliers with no bar named, by counsel's word), CONTEXT (approvals for computing abroad, bodies not named; home-made supply short of HBM).

Of that supply it says US export controls are an important factor, and it is those it goes on to analyse: "美国出口管制政策是影响高性能算力设备供应的重要因素" [cn-ga31-counter-27]; and it reports no case in which controls stopped it: "公司未发生因出口管制、贸易限制或其他贸易政策变化导致设备无法采购、 客户无法交付或业务无法开展的情形" [cn-ga31-counter-27] (no case in which export controls, trade restrictions or other changes of trade policy left it unable to buy equipment, to deliver to customers or to do business). Like 智微智能's reply above, it names no chip maker for the servers it plans to buy (it names Nvidia in setting out the US rules) and no Chinese approval. Cuts: AGAINST (limit 2: a listed company tells the exchange that buying such servers through suppliers in China breaches no Chinese prohibition, and finds nothing it deals in banned or restricted under China's dual-use catalogue), CONTEXT (another listed buyer of GPU servers beside 智微智能 and Hongjing; supports «not a published ban»).
- **Zhipu, Dec 30, 2025 and Mar 31, 2026 · its prospectus on what chips it bought, and its results on home-made chips** (filings, in the copies this file already cites; byte-identical copies of both from two other hosts, a newspaper's and a broker's file servers, are also held: the same sha256 for each pair). The prospectus, printed page 214, on its purchases before and after it was put on the American Entity List on Jan 16, 2025: "we purchased certain computing hardware containing U.S.-origin AI chips subject to the EAR in compliance with the relevant export control rules and regulations and we have not procured any items subject to the EAR since August 2023" [cn-le29-zhipu-prospectus-en]; "After the Entity List Addition, we are unable to purchase AI chips subject to the EAR and have not procured any AI chips subject to the EAR." [cn-le29-zhipu-prospectus-en]; "this has not had an adverse impact on our business, as we did not plan to develop our own AI data center" [cn-le29-zhipu-prospectus-en]; "In fact, we have not procured any AI chips, whether subject to the EAR or not, since the Entity List Addition. Instead, we procure computing resources from reputable cloud computing service providers in China" [cn-le29-zhipu-prospectus-en]. It names no chip maker and does not say what chips those cloud providers run. The results announcement of Mar 31, 2026, of fitting its GLM models to home-made chips: "This deep tuning has enabled GLM series models to achieve inference efficiency on domestic chips comparable to that of international top-tier chips" [cn-le29-zhipu-results-20260331-en] (the company's own claim, for inference). Neither text names a Chinese authority or a Chinese rule on chips. Cuts: AGAINST (limit 4: the bar it names is American, and it rents its computing by its own choice); CONTEXT (a lab that bought hardware with American AI chips until August 2023 and none of any kind after January 2025).
- **Nov 10, 2024 and March 2025 · 厦门弘信电子科技集团股份有限公司 (弘信电子, 300657), which builds computing clusters in Qingyang, Gansu, and rents out computing: its filed investor record and its annual report for 2024** (filings, cninfo). Asked whether its computing cards are mainly Nvidia's and Enflame's, it said yes; its customers still mostly want imported chips, and it steers them toward the home-made one: "请问公司当前算力卡是不是以英伟达和燧原为主？答：是的。客户需求方面，英伟达的卡还是比较受欢迎" [cn-ga31-xjqhplans-66]; "我们也在引导客户更多使用国产燧原芯片" [cn-ga31-xjqhplans-66]; "根据客户需求，目前绝大多数还是进口高性能芯片" [cn-ga31-xjqhplans-66]; "相信未来国产替代也是个大趋势" [cn-ga31-xjqhplans-66]. Its annual report plans a mixed cluster for 2025: "在甘肃庆阳建设国产+英伟达混合异构算力集群" [cn-ga31-xjqhplans-65]. Both pages name the national strategy «东数西算» (east data, west computing) for its building at Qingyang [cn-ga31-xjqhplans-65] [cn-ga31-xjqhplans-66]; for its choice of chips neither names a Chinese authority, rule or approval. Cuts: FOR the companies' side of the question (customers ask for Nvidia cards), and FOR the direction (a builder steering customers to home-made chips; it names no authority for doing so); a plan, not a purchase.
- **Nov 19, 2026 · Alibaba Group Holding Limited, its results announcement as filed in Hong Kong, the English version, in the copy on the company's own site** (a filing: "ANNOUNCEMENT OF THE SEPTEMBER QUARTER 2026 RESULTS AND INTERIM RESULTS FOR THE SIX MONTHS ENDED SEPTEMBER 30, 2026" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own25-alibabagroup], signed by order of the board, "Hong Kong, November 19, 2026" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own25-alibabagroup]). Its note on events after the period is worded more firmly in English than in the Chinese version of the same announcement: the American rule of Nov 16, 2026 «will impact» the company's ability to access compute from certain providers outside China, where the Chinese version has «may affect». Words, under the heading SUBSEQUENT EVENTS: "On November 16, 2026, the Bureau of Industry and Security of the U.S. Department of Commerce issued an interim final rule addressing remote access to advanced semiconductors (the “Rule”)." [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own25-alibabagroup]; "The Rule introduces tighter controls on the export of advanced semiconductors to certain countries and data center operators." [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own25-alibabagroup]; "The Rule, as written, will impact our company's ability to access compute from certain providers outside China." [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own25-alibabagroup]; "We are evaluating the impact of the Rule, which may still be subject to change, and an estimate of the financial effect cannot be reasonably made as of the date of this results announcement." [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own25-alibabagroup]. The Chinese version, signed "香港，2026 年 11 月 19 日" [cn-le07-baba-2026q3-results], in the same place: "該規則（按現有條文）可能會影響本公司從某些中國以外提供商獲取計算資源的能力" [cn-le07-baba-2026q3-results]. The note is not in the company's press release of the same day, datelined "Hong Kong, China, November 19, 2026" [cn-le07-baba-2026q3-results-en], which is the English text held before: its two saved copies are one file (equal bytes, one sha256) [cn-le07-baba-2026q3-results-en] [cn-ga31-tencentcall-19], and a script finds the heading and each of the four sentences in the filed announcement and in neither copy of the release (control: it finds «Zhenwu V900» in all three; among the 6,033 saved pages of this project that had a text file on Nov 25, 2026, the words «will impact our company» stand in this one alone, by a count that finds «Zhenwu V900» in 12). The note does not say which providers, where, or how much. It names no Chinese authority. Cuts: AGAINST (limit 4: in a filing, the company names an American rule as what will impact, in the Chinese version may affect, its ability to get computing from certain providers outside China); CONTEXT (the English «will» against the Chinese «may»: the two versions of one filing differ in strength).
- **Nov 16 to 24, 2026 · Third-quarter filings and releases of nine more companies: what they say on chips and supply** (filings in their Chinese versions as a Chinese financial site's file host carries them, and releases as wire and news sites carry them, one from its company's own investor site; the companies' own words). SenseTime, voluntary announcement on the nine months to Sept 30, 2026, by order of the board, "香港，2026年11月18日" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own7-dfcfw-zh]: "2026 年7 月，本集團在行業內率先將主流模型在國產芯片上的混合推理推向規模化商用" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own7-dfcfw-zh] (in July 2026 the group was the first in its industry to take mixed inference of mainstream models on home-made chips to commercial scale); "本集團在多卡型、多場景及多客戶中推進國產化替代" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own7-dfcfw-zh] (the group is advancing home-made substitution across card types, uses and customers). It gives no reason for the substitution and names no authority; the English update on its own site, held before [cn-ga31-q3own-37], has neither sentence. Xiaomi, results announcement, "2026 年 11 月 24 日" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own24-dfcfw-zh]: three chips of its own announced in August 2026, "三顆芯片均完成回片驗證" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own24-dfcfw-zh] (all three have completed validation of the chips back from fabrication), one of them "玄戒O100 為高帶寬AI加速芯片" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own24-dfcfw-zh] (a high-bandwidth AI accelerator chip); the announcement places them in a phone, a tablet, driving and a prototype device that runs models locally; it has no word for a data centre or a server (counted: 數據中心 0, 服務器 0). Kingsoft Cloud, release of Nov 24, 2026, its chief financial officer: "the RMB6.2 billion quarterly cash capital expenditure was nearly 100% covered by our operating cash flows, same as in the previous quarter" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own2-prnewswire]; the release's own text has no chip word (the wire site's menu aside), and the Chinese version of its Hong Kong announcement, dated "香港，二零二六年十一月二十四日" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own22-dfcfw-zh], has none either. VNET, release of Nov 23, 2026: "Taking into account the Company's year-to-date capital expenditures and disciplined capital allocation approach, the Company now expects capital expenditure to be in the range of RMB8 billion to RMB10 billion for the full year of 2026." [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3results4-prnewswire]; the release gives no earlier range and no word on chip supply (the question was put on its call: the entry on the calls of Nov 16 to 24 in section 3.5). Baidu, results of Nov 16, 2026, in the release held before and in the Chinese version of its Hong Kong announcement, now saved [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own12-dfcfw-zh]: revenue from its GPU cloud "increased by 191% year over year and 43% quarter over quarter in the third quarter of 2026" [cn-le07-baidu-2026q3-results]. Kuaishou, release of Nov 19, 2026: a recommendation model "increases user time spent on short-video content without requiring additional inference computing resources" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3results20-macaubusiness]. Bilibili's release of Nov 24 and iQIYI's of Nov 16 have no chip word [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own10-irbilibili] [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own11-menafn]; Unisound's announcement of Nov 23 names chips only as its own chips for devices and as the subject of a coding test [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own52-dfcfw-zh]. The record's check for a Chinese authority beside a chip word (the same patterns, a window of 200 characters, the same three controls), run over these twelve saved texts with Alibaba's filed announcement (the entry above) and Unisound's news page [cn-ga31-unsaved3-q3own45] among them, and over three results releases held since Nov 23 that no earlier list named (JD.com's, NetEase's and Baidu's [cn-le07-jd-2026q3-call] [cn-le07-ntes-2026q3-results]): an authority word with a chip word 0 in 15; a government word with chip and restriction words 0; Nvidia named 0. The only rule that stands beside a chip word in the 15 is the American one in Alibaba's note. Controls behave (the cyberspace regulator is found on the People's Daily commentary and in Alibaba's annual report, Beijing on Bloomberg's page). What these copies are not: the English versions of the Hong Kong filings of Baidu, Xiaomi, SenseTime, Unisound and Kingsoft Cloud, and the copies on the SEC's site, were not reached. Cuts: AGAINST (limit 4: SenseTime gives home-made substitution as its own course, with no authority named); CONTEXT (spending and demand).
- **Oct 14 and 28, 2025 and Apr 21, 2026 · Three Hong Kong listing prospectuses on China's import duty on chips and on the chips' origin: one says its US-origin chips of two kinds were exempt from Apr 24, 2025 and that it incurred the duty on memory chips; one applies the wafer rule of origin to an Nvidia chip by name** (filings: the Chinese versions of the prospectuses as a Chinese financial site's file host carries them; the English versions were not reached). Fibocom Wireless (深圳市廣和通無線股份有限公司), which calls itself a provider of wireless communication modules, prospectus of Oct 14, 2025: "就基帶芯片及射頻芯片而言，自2025 年4 月24 日至最後實際可行日期，我們於日常業務過程中採購的美國原產基帶芯片及射頻芯片獲豁免中國關稅" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext89-dfcfw-a] (for baseband and radio-frequency chips, from Apr 24, 2025 to the latest practicable date, the US-origin chips it bought in the ordinary course were exempt from Chinese tariffs); "就存儲芯片而言，自2025年4月10日至最後實際可行日期，我們採購美國原產存儲芯片人民幣7.1百萬元，並就美國原產進口產品產生中國關稅總額約人民幣0.7百萬元" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext89-dfcfw-a] (for memory chips, from Apr 10, 2025 to the latest practicable date it bought 7.1 million yuan of US-origin memory chips and incurred Chinese tariffs on US-origin imports of about 0.7 million yuan in all); the latest practicable date is "2025 年10 月6 日" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext89-dfcfw-a]. The prospectus names no announcement for the exemption from Apr 24. The date and the split (other chips exempt, memory chips taxed) are those of the press reports of Apr 25, 2025 of a waiver that customs did not publish (Tier 2, context). Pony AI, which calls itself a self-driving technology company, prospectus of Oct 28, 2025: "據我們所知，我們採購的NVIDIA Orin-X芯片的晶圓製造設施位於台灣地區或韓國" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext91-dfcfw-a] (to its knowledge the wafers of the NVIDIA Orin-X chips it buys are made in Taiwan or South Korea); "「集成電路」原產地按四位數稅號變更原則認定，以晶圓流片地點認定為原產國" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext91-dfcfw-a]; "因此，我們採購的NVIDIA Orin-X芯片須繳納0%的關稅" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext91-dfcfw-a] (so the chips bear a 0% tariff). It rests the origin on the customs order on origin and on the semiconductor association's notice of Apr 11, 2025, and the 0% rate on the tariff schedule, under which Taiwan and South Korea qualify for most-favoured-nation treatment; it dates the customs order "於2024年10月28日頒佈並自2024年12月1日起生效" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext91-dfcfw-a], where customs' own explanation gives 2005 (section 5): whether the prospectus means an amended text was not checked. Pony AI lists the chip among raw materials it imports for its self-driving business; the passage is not about data-centre processors. Shanghai Sunmi Technology (上海商米科技集團股份有限公司), which calls itself a provider of commercial internet-of-things solutions, prospectus of Apr 21, 2026: "我們直接或間接採購若干美國原產的中低端商用芯片（如CPU、USB芯片及接口芯片），該等芯片一般不受美國對中國施加的出口限制，並獲豁免遵守對中國的進口關稅" [cn-ga31-unsaved3-customstext92-dfcfw-a] (it buys, directly or indirectly, some lower and middle grade commercial chips of US origin, such as CPUs, USB chips and interface chips, which in general are not under American export limits on China and are exempt from the import tariffs). None of the three names an announcement, a licence or a permit as the ground of an exemption (one cites the customs order on origin, one a joint tax announcement of 2019 in its survey of tax rules), and none of the passages speaks of graphics processors or AI accelerators for data centres. Cuts: CONTEXT (limit 2: a filing states the effect that the press reported in April 2025 from unnamed importers, an exemption from Apr 24, 2025 with memory chips left out; no customs text of it has been found. And the rule that a chip's origin is where its wafer was made is applied in a filing to an Nvidia chip by name).
- **2026 · Zhipu (Knowledge Atlas Technology), two more sentences of filings this file already cites: unit inference cost down 80% on its cluster of home-made chips, and money paid ahead to suppliers for computing** (filings, in the copies held from the company's investor-site vendor: the interim results for the first half of 2026, whose address carries 20260831, and the annual report for 2025, whose address carries 20260419). The interim results, after the sentence already quoted on home-made chips as a primary inference resource (the number of chips is already in section 3.5, from the Chinese version's «10萬級»): "It has achieved large-scale, low-cost inference across a cluster of over 100,000 domestic chips, with unit Token inference costs declining by 80% from the beginning of the year." [cn-le29-zhipu-2026-interim-results-en]. The annual report, in the note on other non-current assets, in thousands of yuan at the end of 2025 and of 2024: "Prepayments for computing service fee and others 209,178 166,893" [cn-le29-zhipu-2025-annual-en]; "The prepayments for computing service fee represented payments made to suppliers to secure these suppliers' computing services for a certain period, and will be deducted by subsequent utilisation of the computing services from these suppliers." [cn-le29-zhipu-2025-annual-en]. Neither passage says whose chips the 100,000 are, where the cluster is, or which suppliers were paid ahead. Cuts: AGAINST (limit 4: a model maker on the American Entity List gives a number, on the record, for home-made chips in use for inference and for the fall in its unit cost, and the reason it gives is its own cost curve), CONTEXT (it pays ahead to secure computing).

### 3.5 Executives of China's AI companies, on results calls and in public

(Tencent, Baidu, Kingsoft Cloud, Meituan, Kuaishou and Xiaomi transcripts are a stock-data site's, with speaker labels not checked against the recordings; Alibaba's are its own. Full table: tables/labs_said.tsv (57 rows) and tables/labs_said_more.tsv (23 rows).)

- **Tencent, Mar 19 and May 14, 2025 · Martin Lau, president.** Tencent stepped up GPU buying from late 2024 and said its stockpile of chips should be enough to train models for a few more generations. Words: "Since the fourth quarter of 2024, we have stepped up our purchase of GPUs" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20250319-sa]; "we should have enough high-end chips to continue our training of models for a few more generations going forward" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20250514-sa]. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Tencent, Aug 13, 2025 · Martin Lau** (against-row 29 of Tier 2 already has the words). He never names the H20 or Nvidia; «no plan to keep buying the H20» is a Chinese outlet's conclusion, not his words: DoNews, "从刘炽平的表态来看，即便目前英伟达H20和AMD MI308已经恢复了对华出口，但是腾讯目前并不会继续采购。" [cn-le09-donews-tencent-call-5950436] (judging from Lau's remarks, even though exports of Nvidia's H20 and AMD's MI308 to China have now resumed, Tencent will not for now keep buying), crediting foreign media. Caixin's account matches the transcript: "腾讯拥有足够的芯片储备用于模型的训练和升级" [cn-counter-b24-4]. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Tencent, Mar 18, 2026 · Martin Lau.** Words: "you actually want to have access to the most state-of-the-art, you know, training chips to the extent possible" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20260318-sa]; "given, you know, the constraints in the supply chain and all the different regulations, right, sometimes we just have to rent" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20260318-sa]. Cuts: FOR (what the company wants).
- **Tencent, May 13, 2026 · James Mitchell, chief strategy officer, as the stock-data site and Sina Tech's Chinese record label him; Xueqiu's Chinese transcript gives the same answer to the chief financial officer, "发言人：罗硕瀚" [cn-ga31-tencentcall-25]** (adds to the results-calls row above). After the "policy restrictions" line he says Tencent has not faced such artificial constraints on CPUs or networking chips, by contrast with GPUs; he does not say whose. Words: "we haven't faced those sort of artificial additional constraints on CPU or networking chips" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20260513-sa]; "until now we actually haven't had sufficient GPUs to begin to service the external demand" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20260513-sa]. Cuts: FOR.
- **Tencent, May 28 and Jun 5, 2026 · Dowson Tong, senior executive vice president, as reported** (HK01's reported speech for May 28, not a quotation; a direct quotation via Eastmoney for Jun 5). Asked whether Tencent was allowed to buy the H200, he said, in HK01's words, that the authorities very much support bringing in chips from home and abroad, and gave no answer on models; a week later he said he believed no chip maker then had enough capacity to meet the market's demand. Words: "问及是否获准购买英伟达Nvidia H200芯片" [cn-le09-hk01-tencent-tong-domestic-chips]; "官方非常支持引进海内外的芯片产品，但就未可答复具体的芯片型号" [cn-le09-hk01-tencent-tong-domestic-chips] (the authorities very much support bringing in chip products from home and abroad, but he could not answer on specific chip models); "我跟很多芯片厂商、合作伙伴都有打交道，我相信没有一家今天有足够的产能去满足市场的需求。" [cn-le09-eastmoney-tencent-more-chipmakers-20260606]. Infocast's item, now saved from AASTOCKS, gives it in reported speech, as his answer to whether Beijing had approved Tencent's H200 purchases: "對於集團採購不同的芯片，中央政府是表示支持的，集團亦會引入國內外的芯片" [cn-ga31-carriers-8] (as for the group buying different chips, the central government has said it supports this; the group will also bring in chips from home and abroad); "被問到北京是否已批准騰訊採購 H200芯片，他作出上述回應" [cn-ga31-carriers-8]. Two more accounts quote or report him on working with chip makers, international and mainland, without the question on approval or the words on the government's support: Now News, "我們在國內重點會繼續與很多國內芯片廠商保持及擴大合作" [cn-ga31-carriers-19]; Wen Wei Po, "騰訊與多家知名國際晶片廠商有長期合作關係" [cn-ga31-carriers-53]. Cuts: AGAINST (an executive says the authorities backed imports), with that caution.
- **Tencent, Nov 18, 2026 · Martin Lau and James Mitchell, results call.** The new US restrictions set everyone in China back, leaving everyone with less advanced compute whenever they began leasing or building capacity outside China; the next model will probably take longer in the short run; Tencent can no longer offer high-end GPUs abroad; it is exiting contracts it had prepaid, and prepayments may reverse in some quarters; it thinks cloud revenue will not be substantially hurt, because inference, which drives that revenue, can run on domestic chips. Words: "So everyone is actually having less compute, advanced compute to work with, no matter when you actually started leasing or building capacities outside of China." [cn-o36-tencent-call-20261118-sa]; "When we look at the next version, it would take probably longer time for us to come up with the next version in the short run." [cn-o36-tencent-call-20261118-sa]; "When it comes to GPU, then unfortunately, we cannot actually offer high-end GPU anymore." [cn-o36-tencent-call-20261118-sa]; "So actually, we think that our cloud revenue will not be substantially adversely affected by the changes." [cn-o36-tencent-call-20261118-sa]; "over the longer run, we believe all Chinese players would actually have to rely on domestic supply chains and domestic chips" [cn-o36-tencent-call-20261118-sa]. Cuts: FOR (the company sought foreign compute abroad) and AGAINST «only Beijing» (the barrier named is American).
- **Alibaba, Mar to Nov 2026 · Eddie Wu, chief executive (the company's own transcripts).** Words: May 13, 2026, "gross margins on mainstream foreign AI chips are very high, basically in the 60% to 80% range" [cn-o36-alibaba-call-20260513-own]; Aug 20, 2026, "I also do not think there is a so-called government-led compute supply market." [cn-o36-alibaba-call-20260820-own]; Mar 19, 2026, "Because domestic chips still lag behind their foreign counterparts in manufacturing process nodes and overall performance" [cn-o36-alibaba-call-20260319-own]. Nov 19, 2026 (stock-data site; Wu spoke Chinese, an interpreter labelled Speaker 4): "domestic compute will contribute a higher proportion of our overall compute and will become the primary engine supporting cloud revenue growth, subsequently lowering dependence on overseas compute" [cn-o36-alibaba-call-20261119-sa]; "the new compute export control rules will affect next year's products and services" [cn-o36-alibaba-call-20261119-sa]; "we might expect our growth target for cloud revenue to be in the 30%-45% range" [cn-o36-alibaba-call-20261119-sa]. Cuts: AGAINST (reasons of its own; US rules named), and FOR (it had relied on overseas compute).
- **Alibaba, Nov 19, 2026 · Eddie Wu, as National Business Daily reported** (against-row 10 of Tier 2). He judged that from 2027 the great majority of added AI chip supply would come mainly from T-Head's own home-made chips, and that it would also buy other home-made chips for some inference. Words: "结合当前形势，他判断2027年及之后，公司绝大部分新增AI芯片供给将以自研平头哥国产芯片为主" [cn-labs-alibaba-nov-supply]; "同时也会采购其他国产芯片，用来满足内部部分推理需求" [cn-labs-alibaba-nov-supply]. Cuts: AGAINST.
- **Baidu, Feb 2024 and May 2026 · Dou Shen and Robin Li.** Words: "On supply, domestic AI chips still face near-term challenges around the capacity and the supply chain maturity." [cn-o36-baidu-call-20260518-sa]; "Inference is an area where domestic chips can be highly relevant and competitive." [cn-o36-baidu-call-20260518-sa]; Feb 2024, Robin Li: "using less advanced chips for efficient model training and inferencing" [cn-against2-baidu-efficient-stack-20240229]. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Kingsoft Cloud, 2025 to 2026.** Words: May 28, 2025 (an interpreter for the chief executive), "this is actually not the first time for similar kind of restrictions to be imposed on chips" [cn-le09-kingsoftcloud-call-20250528-sa]; Aug 20, 2025, chief executive Zou Tao, "there's a chance that the domestic chip supply will not be able to meet the demand in the market in China" [cn-le09-kingsoftcloud-call-20250820-sa]; Aug 19, 2026, senior vice president Kaiyan Tian (through an interpreter), "The supply difficulty is actually a long-term kind of situation." [cn-le09-kingsoftcloud-call-20260819-sa]. Cuts: FOR (a reason to want foreign chips), CONTEXT.

### 3.6 The state telecom operators' own purchases (their own notices, mostly as tender sites carry them, one filing and one news page; winners' own filings and pages; trade press and brokers' reports)

- **2025 to 2026 · China Mobile.** Its 2025 inference purchase had four lots of CUDA-like equipment worth over 1.7 billion yuan and two of CANN-ecosystem equipment (CANN is the software Reuters calls Huawei's "proprietary CANN software system" [cn-ga10-huawei-rthk-20260327]) worth about 3.4 billion yuan; its Mar 2026 tender sought about 6,208 cards of CANN-ecosystem supernodes; Kunlunxin said it would supply CUDA-compatible chips to H3C and ZTE (that this covers the CUDA-like lots is the project's inference). Words: "本次集采金额超过50亿元，共分为6个标包。其中，标包1、标包2、标包3和标包4均为类CUDA生态设备，总含税价超17亿元；标包5和标包6为CANN生态设备，总含税价约34亿元。" [cn-o35-c114-cmcc-inference-2025]; "采购产品为人工智能超节点设备（CANN生态），采购规模约为6208卡（776套计算节点）。" [cn-le17-c114-w3542-1307004]; "Kunlunxin will supply artificial intelligence chips compatible with Nvidia's CUDA software platform to firms such as H3C Technologies and telecoms equipment maker ZTE" [cn-le17-standard-kunlunxin-cmcc]. The chip is not stated for its 7,994-server purchase of 2024, its 20,480-card and 52,160-card supernode tenders, or China Telecom's N-series tender for an estimated 5,248 servers: "中国移动此次采购人工智能超节点合计52160卡，计划选取6-7家中标人。" [cn-le17-c114-w118-1321457]; "其中智算服务器规模预估为5248台。" [cn-le17-c114-w3543-1321162]. China Telecom's 2023 to 2024 I-series lots (two of the four in a broker's table) were specified at an FP16 power per card of not less than "300TFLOPS（H800）" [cn-counter-b11-3]. Cuts: FOR (the operators' side of the Shanghai reply, now in their own purchases).

The chip designer, which calls itself an AI chip company that China Mobile has invested in, says that in the CUDA-like section of that central purchase it took the largest share in three lots. - **Aug 30, 2024 and Mar 25, 2025 · The state operators' computing: China Mobile's Harbin centre, 100% home-made AI chips; four 10,000-card clusters in service** (a provincial newspaper's report on the Heilongjiang government's site, the page address dated August 2024; Xinhua's report of what the state-assets regulator said). China Mobile's intelligent-computing centre in Harbin, described as the largest single cluster of any operator, went into service with a home-made AI-chip rate of 100%; by Mar 25, 2025 the operators had built and put into service four 10,000-card clusters (no chip maker named in that report). Words: "全球运营商最大单集群智算中心在哈尔滨建成投用" [cn-ga31-early2024-69]; "中国移动智算中心(哈尔滨)AI芯片国产化率100%" [cn-ga31-early2024-69]; "记者3月25日从国务院国资委获悉" [cn-ga31-early2024-30]; "运营商建成投产4个万卡集群" [cn-ga31-early2024-30]. Cuts: FOR (the state operators' side of the split, in 2024, on a government site), the operators being state firms, not the AI companies of the question.
- **2024 to 2026 · A carrier's own tender, its suppliers' filings and statements, and China Unicom's agent's notices** (documents and companies' own pages; table tables/procurement_notices_sweep.tsv). China Mobile's signed tender of Apr 18, 2024 (CMCC20240500088, a publicly hosted copy of the PDF) names no chip: "采购人工智能服务器7994台及配套产品、白盒交换机 60台" [cn-ga31-tenders-42]. One supplier group, Digital China through three subsidiaries, names the chip of its own share in three China Mobile purchases, in the first two as one of the winning candidates. Shenzhou Kuntai (Xiamen) of that 2024-2025 purchase, lot 1 (10.53%), and Digital China (China) of the 2023-2024 trial network, lot 12 (8.48%; the product a Shenzhou Kuntai server): "本次中标的产品为神州鲲泰KunTai A924 系列AI训练服务器" [cn-ga31-tenders-40], "是基于鲲鹏+昇腾主板打造的AI训练服务器" [cn-ga31-tenders-48]; and of the 2026-2027 supernode purchase (CMCC20260500251, 20,480 cards), on its own page: "中标金额13.17亿元，中标设备搭载共计2048张昇腾950DT" [cn-ga31-tenders-4]; its board's filing gives the share, "项目招标总规模为20,480 卡" [cn-ga31-tenders-5], and no chip. Dahua's own filing for another winning candidate, its subsidiary, which had signed no contract, gives "中标份额4096卡，交货期30天" [cn-ga31-tenders-46] and no chip. China Unicom's agent (中招国际): 2025 AI servers (TC250P19E) and 2026 computing-pool equipment (TC260P0TW), candidates and opening bids only, no chip printed; first in the second, "设备整机及集成服务模型投标总价154,405,861.41元（不含税）" [cn-ga31-tenders-9], is a consortium led by Huawei Technical Services. Unicom Data Intelligence is choosing a supplier to rent it a «910B» computing cluster, in a notice of the chosen candidate that writes neither Ascend nor Huawei (that 910B is Huawei's Ascend chip is general knowledge, not the page's word): "2026年联通数智910B智算集群算力租赁服务采购项目" [cn-ga31-tenders-7] (a reprint). A share named by its winner is not the lot; the carriers' own result notices for these lots are still not saved (their sites gave no readable page). Cuts: FOR (one supplier group's shares on Ascend, by its own word; a carrier unit seeking to rent a 910B cluster), CONTEXT (no chip printed in the carriers' and agent's notices).
- **Jul 2024 to Nov 2026 · What the carriers' own notices and filings say about the chips in their computing purchases** (documents: the carriers' and their agents' notices, mostly in reprints on tender sites, a China Telecom filing and a China Telecom news page; table tables/carriers_own_notices.tsv). Home-made by name. China Mobile's Yunnan company (中国移动通信集团云南有限公司), tender of Feb 26, 2026 (on its agent's own site), for a customer's 300P computing project: bidders must also hold the chip maker's authorization, "投标人还应同时取得GPU芯片制造商针对本项目的原厂授权" [cn-ga31-operators-63], and "本次项目应为国内主流GPU芯片制造商，包括海思（昇腾）、海光、景嘉微、寒武纪、平头哥、昆仑芯、天数智芯、摩尔线程、壁仞科技、燧原、登临、沐曦等" [cn-ga31-operators-63] (the maker must be a mainstream domestic GPU maker, these twelve named). China Mobile's Ningxia branch, Sept 21, 2026, in a tender site's short account of the notice (not the notice itself): "2026年-2027年国产算力服务器采购项目" [cn-ga31-operators-88] (home-made computing servers). China Telecom's Shaanxi branch, Oct 28, 2026 (a masked reprint): "配置32台平头哥真武M890 GPU服务器，每台8张平头哥真武M890 OAM加速卡，共256卡" [cn-ga31-operators-31] (T-Head's Zhenwu M890). China Telecom's Guangdong branch, Feb 2026, a «国产化万卡智算池» (home-made ten-thousand-card AI computing pool), phase two, and China Telecom's own interim report of 2026 lists it among its construction in progress, with 816,202,918.55 yuan added in the half year to Jun 30, 2026, 93.37% of its budget, from its own funds: "2026年广东公司国产化万卡智算池二期建设项目" [cn-ga31-operators-100]. China Telecom's own news page, dated Apr 27, 2025, of an event the day before: "全球首个商用智算昇腾超节点在中国电信粤港澳大湾区（韶关）算力集群正式发布" [cn-ga31-operators-66]. Ecosystem labels in China Mobile's own notices: "标包6（CANN生态-扣卡）" [cn-ga31-operators-7] (Jun 2025), "标包1（类CUDA生态PCIe卡）" [cn-ga31-operators-32] (Aug 2025), "人工智能超节点设备（CANN生态）" [cn-ga31-operators-19] (Mar 2026); CANN is the software Reuters calls Huawei's "proprietary CANN software system" [cn-ga10-huawei-rthk-20260327], and «类CUDA» (CUDA-like) names no maker. Origin: China Telecom's tender form carries a clause on where the products come from, in a wider and a narrower form. Wider, in its prequalification notices of Jul 5, 2024 for servers of 2024 and 2025: "3.3.1投标产品应是来自中华人民共和国或是与中华人民共和国有正常贸易与往来的国家或地区的产品" [cn-ga31-operators-83] (from China or from countries or regions trading normally with China), and the same in Jiangsu's tender published on Sept 30, 2026 [cn-ga31-operators-103]. Narrower in two tenders. In Guangdong's tender of Feb 2026 for the home-made ten-thousand-card pool, the copy's markup sets the words «或是与中华人民共和国有正常贸易与往来的国家或地区» not to display, so the clause a reader sees asks for products from the PRC only [cn-ga31-operators-39] (the saved text file holds the hidden words too, and a search finds them). In the N-series supernode tender of Nov 2026: "2.3.1投标产品应是来自中华人民共和国的产品" [cn-ga31-operators-132] (products from the PRC), asking for "模组形态、NPU架构" [cn-ga31-operators-132] chips and naming no maker; this copy, listed by a tender site on Nov 17, 2026, masks the tender's number and leaves its dates, contract term and price ceiling blank, but prints the estimated quantities, "智算服务器 详见第五章技术规范书 5248台" [cn-ga31-operators-132] with 110 storage units and 374 RoCE switches, and two models of supernode, whose largest number of cards is to be at least 1,024 and at least 256; the wider words stand nowhere in it, shown or hidden, so whether they were an unselected option of the original form or were dropped by the copy cannot be told from it; the trade site C114 dates the tender's start to Nov 16 and gives an estimated 5,248 servers [cn-le17-c114-w3543-1321162]. A clause on the origin of the product is not a clause on the chip inside it (section 2.2). China Unicom's Oct 2025 AI-server tender still lets a foreign-registered maker bid through an agent: "制造商为注册地在中华人民共和国境外（包括香港、澳门、台湾地区）的企业，并且必须通过代理商完成本采购项目的投标活动与合同签署的" [cn-ga31-operators-27]. Nvidia by name: none of these carrier pages (30 saved pages of notices, 25 of them the notices themselves and 5 tender or data sites' records of them, with the filing and the news page) holds 英伟达, NVIDIA or an Nvidia model (script count; control: the same count, on each page as a visitor sees it, finds 英伟达 4 and H800 8 on a relay of China Mobile Shanghai's 2023 tender for a customer's H800 centre, "本工程新建千卡级H800智算中心" [cn-ga31-operators-40]). One other notice held does name Nvidia cards, which it writes «NV»: a Jiangsu company that bears China Unicom's name and publishes its tender on China Unicom's procurement site (the notice names no owner), in a tender dated Sept 23, 2024 to rent computing, "标包三：A100算力服务，包括NV Tesla A100 GPU*1及配套CPU、存储、网络等服务" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14] (this section's entry of that date); a small rental, not one of the central purchases. China Mobile's own tender for the 20,480-card supernode purchase names no chip: "采购人工智能超节点合计20480卡" [cn-ga31-operators-10]. What only others say: the chip in a winner's share (Digital China, FiberHome, Kunlunxin: this section's rows above and tables/procurement_notices_sweep.tsv), brokers' tables, and a China Telecom project head in Qingyang quoted by the Gansu press (Jun 2024): "目前，庆阳算力中心已完成英伟达H800、H100、A800、4090以及国产华为H910B等2000台GPU服务器的部署工作" [cn-ga31-operators-42]. Cuts: FOR (the carriers' own notices ask for home-made chips by maker, or label their lots by software ecosystem; two China Telecom tenders ask for products from the PRC only), CONTEXT (a copy on a tender site is not the carrier's page; China Telecom's tenders of Jul 2024 and Sept 2026 and Unicom's of Oct 31, 2025 still admitted foreign-made products).
- **Sept 23, 2024 · A Jiangsu company that bears China Unicom's name, in its own tender, asks to rent computing on three Nvidia cards named by model** (document: the tender notice of 联通（江苏）产业互联网有限公司 and its tender agent 中邮通建设咨询有限公司, tender number JSCH-G-K1-2024-32, dated Sept 23, 2024 beside a signature line left blank; a PDF copy on a third party's file server, not the company's own site). The notice names no owner of the company; it says it is published on "中国联通采购与招标网" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14] (China Unicom's procurement and tendering site) and sends bidders to register on "中国联通合作方自服务门户" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14] (China Unicom's self-service portal for partners). The notice, for the elastic rental of AI computing, has three lots, each named by a card. Words: "标包一：3090算力服务，包括NV RTX 3090 GPU*1及配套CPU、存储、网络等服务" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14]; "标包二：4090算力服务，包括NV RTX 4090 GPU*1及配套CPU、存储、网络等服务" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14]; "标包三：A100算力服务，包括NV Tesla A100 GPU*1及配套CPU、存储、网络等服务" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14]; "采购预算：800000元（不含税）" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14]; "项目资金由招标人自筹，资金已落实" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14]; "投标人必须为中国境内依法注册的独立法人或依法成立的其他组织" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14]; "招标人为联通（江苏）产业互联网有限公司，招标代理机构为中邮通建设咨询有限公司" [cn-ga31-carrierown-14]. The notice writes «NV», not 英伟达 or NVIDIA; that «NV» stands for Nvidia is a reading from the model names, which is general knowledge and not the page's word. It asks to rent computing by the day under price ceilings; it buys no card, names no supplier and says nothing of where the cards are. It is the one notice of a company bearing a carrier's name, among those now saved, that names Nvidia cards after 2023 (the count in this section's entry on the carriers' own notices was of 30 other pages): a rental with a budget of 800,000 yuan, not one of the ten central purchases. Cuts: AGAINST a uniform rule in the carriers' own buying in 2024 (limit 1); CONTEXT (a small rental of computing, about a year before the reported instructions of 2025; a tender is not a delivery).

The supplementary purchase (一年期, 补充采购). Its result notice, signed "招标人/招标代理机构：中国移动通信有限公司/北京煜金桥通信建设监理咨询有限责任公司" [cn-ga31-operators-49] and dated "2026年01月23日" [cn-ga31-operators-49], names three winners for "标包1（CANN生态-PCIe卡）" [cn-ga31-operators-49]: "河南昆仑技术有限公司" [cn-ga31-operators-49], "武汉长江计算科技有限公司" [cn-ga31-operators-49] and "四川华鲲振宇智能科技有限责任公司" [cn-ga31-operators-49]; it prints no shares. - **Oct 31, 2025 to Jul 24, 2026 · China Unicom's two AI-server tenders: the 2025 result as indexed and as one winner tells it, and who may bid in 2026** (the carrier's and its agent's notices as tender sites carry them; a tender site's index of the result, names masked; the page of one winner's parent company, FiberHome). The 2025 tender of Unicom Digital Technology and three affiliates (TC250P19E): "采购规模合计 168 台智算服务器、 49 台组网交换机" [cn-ga31-operators-27]; "两名中标人的中标份额为：60%、40%" [cn-ga31-operators-27]. A tender site's index of the result shows the two shares with the date of award, "中标份额: 60" [cn-ga31-operators-112] and "中标日期: 2025-12-18" [cn-ga31-operators-112]. FiberHome's own page (its address dated Nov 25, 2025) says its unit Yangtze Computing won "金额约1.06亿元，份额占项目总预算40%" [cn-ga31-operators-36], with servers "搭载先进的CPU、NPU处理器" [cn-ga31-operators-36] (it names no chip maker). The 2026 tender (TC260P0TW; documents on sale from Jul 24, 2026) asks for "人工智能服务器 123 台" [cn-ga31-operators-13] and requires that the bidder be "在中华人民共和国境内登记注册" [cn-ga31-operators-13] and that "本项目人工智能服务器设备、集成安装服务不接受代理商投标" [cn-ga31-operators-13] (no bids by agents for the AI servers or the integration and installation services). The 2025 tender's clause that let a maker registered outside the PRC bid through an agent (quoted in the entry above) is not in the 2026 copy: the word 境外 stands 0 times in it (control: the same search finds it in the 2025 copy). Neither notice has a clause on the chip or names one, and each sends the bidder to the fifth chapter of the tender documents for the technical requirements; whether a bidder registered in China may offer servers with foreign-made parts, the notices do not say. Cuts: CONTEXT (no chip in either notice; the 2026 notice narrows who may bid and says nothing of what the servers hold; the index dates the 2025 award Dec 18, while FiberHome's page of Nov 25 already says the result had been published).
- **Jul 5, 2024 · China Telecom's prequalification notices for its server purchase of 2024 and 2025: two lots of GPU servers, one on x86 processors and one on ARM processors held to the government's server standard; the accelerators set by figures, no maker named** (document: thirteen notices of China Telecom and its agent 中捷通信有限公司, each dated Jul 5, 2024, in one PDF held as a copy on a third party's file server). Lot 2: "此标包为标包 2：GPU 服务器（A 系列）" [cn-ga31-operators-83], an estimated 6,295 servers; lot 9: "此标包为标包 9：GPU 服务器（G 系列）" [cn-ga31-operators-83], an estimated 6,840 (the lots' tables; a script finds each number once in the saved text). Lot 2 asks for X86 processors and, in the first of its two models, "采用训练 GPU 模组" [cn-ga31-operators-83] (training GPU modules), in three alternative sets of figures, the first "显存≥80GB HBM2e、访存带宽≥2.0TB/S、FP16 算力≥300TFLOPS" [cn-ga31-operators-83]; its second model asks for "采用推理 GPU 卡，支持数量≥8 块全高全长双宽 GPU 类型" [cn-ga31-operators-83] (inference GPU cards, the server supporting at least eight, of a stated size). Lot 9 asks for ARM processors, "ARMv8 架构、48Cores" [cn-ga31-operators-83], and, in the first of its three models, "采用推理 GPU 模组，单 GPU 芯片参数规格要求：显存 32GB HBM2e、访存带宽 800GB/s、FP16 算力 280 TFLOPS" [cn-ga31-operators-83] (inference GPU modules); its other two models ask for "采用推理 GPU 卡，支持数量≥4 块" [cn-ga31-operators-83] (inference GPU cards, the server supporting at least four), of two stated sizes. Neither lot names a maker or a model of accelerator. Another lot of the same PDF does name a maker of GPU cards, once: lot 3 (general servers, B series) asks in its fourth model for "采用 AMD GPU 卡，支持数量≥2 块全高全长双宽 GPU" [cn-ga31-operators-83] (AMD GPU cards, the server supporting at least two, each full height, full length and double width). Lot 9 adds a sentence the A-series lot does not have: "其中 CPU 应符合《通用服务器政府采购需求标准（2023 版）》第 75 条要求" [cn-ga31-operators-83] (the CPU must meet article 75 of the government purchasing standard for general servers, 2023 edition). The same sentence stands in eight of the thirteen notices, all of them G-series lots (lots 4, 5, 7, 8, 9, 11, 12 and 13; a script's count). That standard is the finance and industry ministries' of December 2023 (section 2.2), whose notice binds Party and government bodies at township level and above, the public institutions directly under them and their departments' institutions that support them, and says of other units "其他单位可不在采购需求中提出此项要求" [cn-policy-server-procurement-2023] (other units may leave this requirement out): a state carrier, not among the bodies the notice holds to that requirement, wrote its CPU article into its own tender. Article 75 is row 75 of the standard's annex, now held: key components such as the CPU and the operating system shall meet the security-and-reliability evaluation; the row names no country of origin, no maker and no home-made word (section 2.2, the entry on what «article 75» says). Both lots carry the wider clause on where products may come from (the entry above). Cuts: FOR (the carrier applies the government's processor standard of its own accord, in the G-series lots, the GPU lot among them); CONTEXT (processors, not AI chips; the accelerators are set by figures and no maker is named; products of countries trading normally with China admitted).

### 3.7 Chinese chip makers' filed revenue (filings; table tables/market_numbers.tsv, family FILING)

- **Cambricon, Hygon, SMIC, quarterly revenue Q1 2023 to Q3 2026** (Q2 2026 is half-year less Q1, a subtraction). Cambricon's revenue went from 25,666,105.86 yuan in Q1 2024 to 3,530,828,294.64 yuan in Q3 2026; its cloud product line from 1,166,278,485.36 yuan in 2024 to 6,476,855,244.59 yuan in 2025; its largest customer took 79.15% of 2024 sales. SMIC's share of revenue from China rose to 90.8% in Q3 2026. ### 3.8 Computing abroad: Datasection's own filings (Tokyo Stock Exchange; Japanese)

- **Jul 2025 to Nov 2026 · Datasection** (filings, and one English press release of its own). Its two large AI data-centre orders of Jul and Oct 2025 came indirectly, through the partner NowNaw Japan, from one customer it describes as among the world's largest cloud providers and does not name; the Japan order was for a centre to open in Osaka, and a Sydney order of Oct 3, 2025 for the use of 1,250 servers with 10,000 Nvidia B200s is from the same customer; the Osaka works were not complete in Oct 2025; at the customer's request the site moved to Inzai, Chiba, and the chips to B300; in Aug 2026 the owner of one overseas site (not named; the filing has the board of its Australian subsidiary join in accepting the termination) gave notice of termination after talks on the change to B300 dragged on; its sales forecast fell from 162,193 to 17,286 million yen. Jinan's big-data bureau (Jul 15, 2026) lists a built Baidu Kunlun cluster of 10,000 cards; Zhengzhou's industry bureau (posted Sept 2, 2026, to the member Zhang Chaofeng) says 60,000 home-made accelerator chips started running at the national supercomputing internet's core node in April 2026. None of these says what the big internet companies' own clusters run on as a class (Jinan's names one built cluster by its chip, Baidu's Kunlun, without saying whose the cluster is); among the replies found, only Shanghai's do (row above). Cuts: FOR (a city calls Nvidia mainstream; a province says smaller firms prefer foreign computing; one city's centre has built 100P on Nvidia chips) and CONTEXT (home-made clusters built in Jinan and Zhengzhou).
- **2025 to Jun 2026 · Local governments' own words: public computing platforms adapted to Nvidia cards, model makers using a western hub, and a weather centre specifying an H200** (documents and named officials). Linyi's city press conference, "2025年12月31日，临沂市人民政府新闻办公室召开" [cn-ga31-cndocs-71]: the city's computing marketplace "建成“算力超市”，统筹全市多元算力资源，适配NVIDIA、华为昇腾等多种类型算力设备，实现按需申请、随用随调、按量付费" [cn-ga31-cndocs-71]. Jiangsu's data bureau and three partners, "江苏省数据基础设施发展报告（2025年）江苏省数据局" [cn-ga31-cndocs-34] (dated June 2026): the provincial dispatch platform is adapted to "适配寒武纪思元 MLU370、MLU290、MLU270，华为 Ascend 310、Ascend 910 和英伟达类型 A100、H800、V100 GPU" [cn-ga31-cndocs-34]. Gansu's "常务副省长程晓波" [cn-ga31-cndocs-61], at the provincial press conference of May 6, 2026 (the government's summary): "大模型方面，有阿里千问、智谱清言、Kimi、DeepSeek等大模型企业，都在使用庆阳的算力" [cn-ga31-cndocs-61] (which chips that computing runs on, he does not say). The Greater Bay Area weather warning centre in Shenzhen, "预警中心服务器数据运算处理显卡采购需求公告" [cn-ga31-cndocs-36] (posted "2025-09-09 17:41:31" [cn-ga31-cndocs-36]), "预算金额：19.5万元" [cn-ga31-cndocs-36], "需为原装正品，核心型号为NVIDIA H200，显存容量不低于141G" [cn-ga31-cndocs-36]: a state body asking for a genuine H200 card (a call for comment on its needs; no award found). Cuts: AGAINST (state bodies serve, adapt to and seek Nvidia hardware), CONTEXT.

## 4. United States government documents

- **US Trade Representative Jamieson Greer**, Council on Foreign Relations, May 26, 2026: "what passes for the private sector in China is interested in the highest technology chips they can get. And the government in China and the party are interested in promoting their domestic production of chips and indigenous innovation. And there's a natural tension ... And you can guess who wins in that situation." [cn-counter-b22-2]
- **The Commerce Secretary, to a Senate appropriations subcommittee**, Apr 22, 2026, asked by Senator Coons how many H200 chips had been licensed: "my understanding is their cloud companies want to buy these chips. ... Chinese central government has not let them as of yet buy the chips because they're trying to keep their investment focused on their own domestic industry. So that's my understanding of what's happening. So we have not sold them chips as of yet." [cn-us-lutnick-0422-rev-transcript-b: a commercial transcript of the hearing, not the committee's print]. Senator Coons's letter to him of May 5, 2026, on the Senate's site, records the same answer: "you responded in the negative, noting that while your department has licensed the export of these semiconductors to China, the PRC government has not allowed the sale" [cn-us-lutnick-0422-coons-letter]. The committee's own caption track carries both answers: to Senator Shaheen at 0:52:30, "I would tell you they have not bought any as of today." [cn-us-lutnick-0422-rev-transcript-b], and the Coons exchange at 1:17:28. No reply from Commerce to the senator was found among his 140 releases of Jan 3 to Nov 19, 2026 (section 8). The committee's own page for the hearing offers the video and his prepared remarks and no transcript [cn-o37-senate-hearing-page-b]; by a speech program's timing the exchange begins at about 1:17:20 in that video (the project's note on the hearing's record; added Nov 23, 2026); machine hearings of the exchange in the committee's own video by three speech programs (run by the project on Nov 25, 2026) have the words quoted here as the commercial transcript has them; not checked by ear. This is the position in one sentence from the official who licenses the chips: the companies want them, the central government has not let them buy. Three limits. He calls it "my understanding", twice. He says "as of yet". And the same letter sets against it a statement by Nvidia's chief executive: "on March 17, 2026, Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang told a group of reporters that Nvidia had received necessary approvals from both the United States and PRC governments", and "Your statements before the committee appear to contradict Huang's comments."
- **The President**, May 15, 2026, aboard Air Force One. The American Presidency Project's transcript: "Q. But they haven't bought any so far? The President. No, because they chose not to. They want to try and develop their own. But it did come up, and I think something could happen on that." Just before: "the H200 is good. China needs it." [cn-clips-trump-may15-wayback, an Internet Archive capture of Jul 11, 2026]; the official text is now held: "No, because they chose not to. They want to try and develop their own." [cn-usdocs-trump-20260515], with "It didn't come up, but, as you know, Jensen was there." [cn-usdocs-trump-20260515] and "I think something could happen on that." [cn-usdocs-trump-20260515]. CNN's transcript gives the first clause to him as well ("They haven't bought any so far, as you know, because they chose not to") [cn-xi-trump-h200-20260515]; the words after "because" are the same in both. His post of Dec 8, 2025, as kept by the American Presidency Project: "I have informed President Xi, of China, that the United States will allow NVIDIA to ship its H200 products to approved customers in China"; "President Xi responded positively!" [cn-clips-followup-3-relay1]. That is the President's account of Xi's answer, not Xi's words; no Chinese readout of it is among the saved pages. And after his meeting with Xi in Busan (Xinhua: "习近平同美国总统特朗普在釜山举行会晤" [cn-xi-busan-20251030]), Oct 30, 2025 (Daily Compilation of Presidential Documents), he recalled saying "That's really between you and Nvidia." [cn-xi-round-g-6] and added "But we're sort of the arbitrator or the referee." [cn-xi-round-g-6]
- **House Foreign Affairs Committee**, release of Nov 19, 2026: "requested sales of 3.4 million AI chips to China, and the approval of more than 879,000 by BIS" [cn-labs-us-chip-licences-20261119]. Set beside Nvidia's statement in its Form 10-Q for the quarter to Jul 26, 2026 that its shipments under the US government's H200 licensing programme were "less than 1% of Data Center revenue" [man-ms-sec-10q-2026-07], this is the nearest thing to a documentary measure of the gap between what the US licensed and what was shipped. The two figures are not netted against each other here: the committee's count covers Nvidia and AMD and more than one chip. The disclosure was made by the full committee (Chairman Mast, under the Export Control Reform Act; approved by unanimous consent), not by its Democratic members on their own (the files sit on their site); the summary is the committee's own, citing a bureau summary dated May 11, 2026. Of the 879,868 approved, 579,868 are Nvidia's and 300,000 AMD's; all 21 approvals read «Approved, not shipped». The table by status is in the new row of section 4.
- **Bureau of Industry and Security**, Nov 16, 2026: a rule "written by and at the direction of the Department of Treasury and the National Security Council" on remote access to advanced chips [cn-clips-corroboration-1]. The rule (Federal Register, Nov 19, 2026) requires a licence for knowingly sending advanced chips for use in a data centre anywhere that gives more than 12,000,000 aggregate TPP to firms headquartered in China, unless the computing is used only for commercial recommender systems; its preamble says an earlier control already covered exports made on behalf of a firm of concern, as when it "was the exclusive or primary (greater than 50 percent of total compute) remote end user of the data center computing capacity" [cn-usdocs-fr-remote-20261119]. So some remote access from third countries was controlled before that date.
- **Apr 22, 2026 · The Commerce Secretary to Senator Shaheen, same hearing as the Commerce Secretary's row above** (commercial transcript; the committee's own caption track, credited to the National Captioning Institute, carries this answer at 0:52:30 and the Coons exchange at 1:17:28). About 25 minutes before the exchange the argument quotes, he said China had not bought any, without giving a reason. Words: "I would tell you they have not bought any as of today." [cn-us-lutnick-0422-rev-transcript-b]. Machine hearings of the answer in the committee's own video, at about 52:23, by three speech programs (run by the project on Nov 25, 2026) have the same words; not checked by ear. Cuts: FOR, weakly.
- **Jul 14, 2026 · Under Secretary Jeffrey Kessler, House Foreign Affairs Committee** (a commercial transcript; YouTube machine captions of the committee's video agree). H200 shipments had begun but were very few; he named no cause, Chinese or American; he called the H200 a chip American companies are not buying. Words: "To my knowledge, uh, yes, but very few." [ms-g17-x3-hearinghighl-979923], answering "As of today, have shipments of the H two hundreds or equivalent chips started to China or Hong Kong?" [ms-g17-x3-hearinghighl-979923]; "there have been minimal, um, exports of any H two hundreds to China so far" [ms-g17-x3-hearinghighl-979923]; "uh a chip that American companies aren't buying right now" [ms-g17-x3-hearinghighl-979923]. Machine hearings of the three answers in the committee's video by three speech programs (run by the project on Nov 25, 2026) have the same words, fillers aside; not checked by ear. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Feb 24, 2026 · David Peters, Assistant Secretary of Commerce for Export Enforcement, House subcommittee** (a commercial transcript). Asked how many H200s had been sold to Chinese end users: "Uh my understanding is that so far none" [ms-g17-x3-firstbranch-22a30d]; machine hearings of the answer in the committee's video by three speech programs (run by the project on Nov 25, 2026) have «My understanding is that so far, none have been sold.»; not checked by ear. No reason given. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Jun 12, 2025 · Under Secretary Kessler, printed hearing record (CHRG-119hhrg62140)** (an official on the record). Commerce's estimate of Huawei's 2025 Ascend production capacity. And we project that most or all of that will be delivered to companies" [ms-g17-x3-govinfo-5def8e]. (His written answers in the same record are in section 9: read from page images.) Cuts: CONTEXT (Huawei's Ascend supply was short in 2025, on Commerce's estimate of its capacity).
- **Jan 28, 2026 · Senator Gillibrand, Senate Armed Services subcommittee (S. Hrg. 119-550)** (a printed record relaying a report it does not name). [cn-le02-govinfo-shrg65604-pdf] ("improved" sic in the print); the general: "General HARTMAN. Senator, I have not studied that question, so I will agree to get back to you with an answer." [cn-le02-govinfo-shrg65604-pdf]. Not an official confirmation of approval. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Mar 24, 2026 · Senator Ricketts and Under Secretary of State DiNanno, Senate Foreign Relations (S. Hrg. 119-429).** The senator relays Nvidia's statement of purchase orders; the State official speaks only of the American side: the President's direction to support the sale, and the licence review. Words: "purchase orders from ‘‘many companies,’’ in quotes." [cn-le02-govinfo-shrg63909-pdf]; "we had direction from the President to support this chip sale to China" [cn-le02-govinfo-shrg63909-pdf]. Cuts: CONTEXT.
- **Jan 14, 2026 · Michael Kratsios, White House science adviser, to a House committee.** Words: "we're not opening the flood gates for the PRC to purchase as many H200s as they want" [cn-usdocs-house-kratsios-20260114]; "The aggregated export of the chips is capped at 50 percent of U.S. customer volume for this specific chip." [cn-usdocs-house-kratsios-20260114]. Cuts: CONTEXT (US limits).
- **Nov 19, 2026 · House Foreign Affairs Committee disclosure: its summary and the bureau's table "as of May 11, 2026"** (documents; made public by Chairman Mast under the Export Control Reform Act at a business meeting, the disclosure approved by unanimous consent; the PDFs sit on the committee Democrats' site). Applications to sell 3,444,160 covered chips (Nvidia's H200 and AMD's MI325X) to PRC companies and to data-centre operators or ultimate consignees that the table names, with an ultimate location abroad (the table gives names, and, in two rows that give China as the chips' ultimate location, a parent: "a wholly owned subsidiary of Tencent Holdings Limited" [cn-usdocs-hfac-decisions-20261119] and one of ByteDance; the committee's summary speaks of Alibaba "and its subsidiaries" and Tencent "and its subsidiaries"); approvals of 879,868 in 21 applications, every one «Approved, not shipped» and with China as its ultimate location (Nvidia 579,868, AMD 300,000, by the project's sums); returned without action, the bureau's RWA, 1,888,950 in 9 rows, including all five applications over 75,000 chips and all 1,118,500 chips for Malaysia, Indonesia and Thailand; submitted 600,342 in 23 rows; rejected 75,000 in 1 (AMD to UCloud); 30 rows (961,500 chips) filed in March and April 2026. Words: "According to a BIS summary provided to the House Foreign Affairs Committee dated May 11, 2026, as of that date, BIS received applications to sell 3,444,160 covered" [cn-usdocs-hfac-summary-20261119]; "948,950 covered integrated circuits for Alibaba and its subsidiaries, 150,000 for Tencent and its subsidiaries, 43,000 for Baidu, and 4,000 for BYD" [cn-usdocs-hfac-summary-20261119]; "For example, NVIDIA applied on December 19, 2025, to export 445,000 H200s to Tencent." [cn-usdocs-hfac-summary-20261119]; "Covered integrated circuits are the following products: NVIDIA's H200 and AMD's MI325X." [cn-usdocs-hfac-summary-20261119]; "The term "PRC companies" includes entities that are located or headquartered in, or the ultimate parent company of which is headquartered in, the People's Republic of China." [cn-usdocs-hfac-summary-20261119]; "H200 License and Equivalent Application Status as of May 11, 2026" [cn-usdocs-hfac-decisions-20261119]; "508,000      RWA        Malaysia" [cn-usdocs-hfac-decisions-20261119]; "Chairman Brian Mast exercised his authorities under the Export Control Reform Act to publicly reveal export control license application information regarding NVIDIA and AMD's AI chip licenses for the People's Republic of China" [cn-labs-us-chip-licences-20261119]; "during a Business Meeting in which the Committee approved by unanimous consent the disclosure of license information from the Bureau of Industry and Security related to the sale of Nvidia H200 and equivalent AMD chips to China" [cn-le04-meeks-remarks-20261119]; RWA on the bureau's own page: "If your original application was returned without action (RWA), provide the Application Control Number." [cn-le04-bis-ear-748]; the bureau's 2023 caution: "The value of approved licenses does not correspond to exports over the period of the data." [cn-le04-bis-statement-hfac-2023b]. These are exporters' applications naming consignees, not buyers' orders or deliveries; «Approved» is a US licence, not a Chinese approval; the Value column is blacked out; no page says why these rows were returned. The table's single Tencent row of Dec 19, 2025 is 75,000, approved; the documents do not say whether that is the 445,000 application. The table's «High-Flyer Asset Management (Zhejiang)» is not tied to DeepSeek by any saved page (section 8). Tables: tables/house_licence_table.tsv (the offshore rows are its rows 26 to 30). Cuts: AGAINST «only Beijing stood in the way» (the US side returned without action 55% of the chips in the applications its summary of May 11, 2026 records, all the offshore ones among them; the committee says that summary is not complete), FOR on demand (the companies kept being named as consignees into April 2026).
- **Apr 16, 2026 and Nov 13, 2026 · House Select Committee on the CCP, two reports that say opposite things** (documents that repeat press reports). Apr 2026: Beijing subsequently approved H200 purchases by Alibaba, Tencent and ByteDance (its note: Reuters, Jan 28, 2026); Nov 2026: the PRC government refused the H200 chips (its notes lead to one Yahoo Finance article on the President's remark of May 15, 2026). Words: "Beijing subsequently approved H200 purchases by Alibaba," [cn-le03-govinfo-buywhat-pdf]; "China is already steering its low-end compute toward domestic chips while using newly licensed H200s to keep its frontier models competitive" [cn-le03-govinfo-buywhat-pdf]; "the PRC government refused the H200 chips, instead pushing its own" [cn-usdocs-select-rigging-game-report]; its note's title, "Trump Says China Is Blocking Nvidia H200 Purchases Despite US" [cn-usdocs-select-rigging-game-report]; the release page's date, "November 13, 2026" [cn-le03-select-rigging-page]. Neither cites a Chinese document for its statement on the H200. Cuts: both ways.
- **March 2026 · US Trade Representative, 2026 National Trade Estimate, p. 108** (a document). China opened Anti-Monopoly Law investigations into US companies, had terminated or suspended them by the end of 2025, and so, in the report's judgment, showed they were pursued for retaliation; it names no company («Nvidia» 0 places; the control «semiconductor» 21). Words: "China initiated several Anti-Monopoly Law investigations into U.S. companies" [cn-le03-ustr-nte-2026]; "China had terminated or suspended these investigations" [cn-le03-ustr-nte-2026]; "Through these actions, China demonstrated that these investigations were being pursued for retaliatory purposes" [cn-le03-ustr-nte-2026]; on Document 79: "this document reportedly sets out timelines for using only secure and controllable ICT products" [cn-le03-ustr-nte-2026]. Whether the Nvidia case was among those meant is not said (section 2.4: the regulator still listed it as enforcement in April 2026). Cuts: AGAINST reading the case as purchase policy; CONTEXT for the fifth limit.
- **Nov 2025 · US-China Economic and Security Review Commission, 2025 report** (a document; its statements on Beijing's restrictions are its own wording of press reports). It reads the Nvidia antitrust move, made in the midst of negotiations as the Google probe was dropped, as a negotiating tactic (source: the Financial Times); elsewhere it repeats Bloomberg and the Financial Times that authorities discouraged H20 purchases and that the regulator "effectively banned" the RTX Pro 6000D (the commission's phrase). Words: "signaling China’s willingness to both exert leverage and ease" [cn-usdocs-uscc-2025-ch1] "pressure as a negotiating tactic" [cn-usdocs-uscc-2025-ch1]; "In August, Chinese authorities discouraged companies from" [cn-usdocs-uscc-2025-ch1]; "In early 2025, China launched antimonopoly reviews into U.S." [cn-le03-uscc-2025-full]; chapter 9: "Chinese officials are increasingly employing similar tactics to curb purchases of leading-edge" [cn-le03-uscc-2025-ch9]. Cuts: AGAINST on motive (leverage); FOR on direction only as press repeated.
- **Nov 18, 2026 · US-China commission, 2026 report, chapter 8 and Appendix II** (a document). - **May 31, 2026 · Bureau of Industry and Security, guidance** (a document). A licence is required, unless a licence exception the guidance cites is available, to export advanced computing items to entities headquartered in China, or whose ultimate parent is, even when they are located abroad. Words: "even if the entities themselves are located outside Country Group D:5 or Macau" [cn-usside-037]; "This license requirement was first introduced on November 17, 2023." [cn-usside-037]. Cuts: CONTEXT (why the consignees abroad in the House table needed licences).
- **Nov 19, 2026 · Bureau of Industry and Security, interim final rule on remote access, Federal Register** (a document). Its stated aim is to undermine the ability of entities headquartered in China and other countries of concern to use American advanced chips to train advanced models in data centres abroad; it names no company, data centre or case; it requires a licence for knowingly sending advanced chips for use in a data centre, or linked data centres, that gives more than 12,000,000 aggregate TPP to or for such entities or those whose ultimate parent is headquartered there («for» taking in a customer known to train models for them or to provide models to them), unless the computing is used only for commercial recommender systems; an earlier control already covered exports made on behalf of such a firm, as when it was the exclusive or primary remote user of a data centre. Words: "These restrictions will undermine the ability of companies headquartered in countries of concern from using U.S. advanced Super Intelligence (SI) semiconductors to train advanced SI models" [cn-usdocs-fr-remote-20261119]; "to more than 12,000,000 ``aggregate TPP''" [cn-usdocs-fr-remote-20261119]; "was the exclusive or primary (greater than 50 percent of total compute) remote end user of the data center computing capacity" [cn-usdocs-fr-remote-20261119]; "Effective date: This rule is effective November 19, 2026." [cn-usdocs-fr-remote-20261119]; the bureau's release of Nov 16: "cannot be circumvented through remote computing arrangements involving data centers in third countries" [cn-usside-072]. Cuts: CONTEXT (limit 4).
